# Switches

Every setting that turns a behaviour on in `lamp-voice`, `lamp-body`, `lamp-eyes`,
`lamp-expression`, `lamp-name`, `lamp-skills`, `lamp-actions`, `lamp-tasks`, `lamp-controls`, `lamp-ears`, `lamp-cloud` and `lamp-mood`: what it does,
what lamp-4ace and the product set, the pull request that added it with the lamp
check it named, and the product default proposed for it. Made for the owner's
question of 17 October, "why off?", and for switching lamp-4ace on group by
group: each switch that passes its check becomes the default in code before
1 November (the first builder, #705, 07:30).

**As of** `lamp-voice` `8c3df0449` (17 October 2026, about 08:50 +07), and the
product column as #1332 (17 October, 15:41) sets it, with `LAMP_SPOKEN_TO`
from #1378. The code is the source of
truth; this page is a census of it, to be kept up as switches are added, turned
on or removed.

## How to read it

- **Values and default:** what the code accepts, and what happens when the
  setting is absent. Almost every switch is off unless set.
- **lamp-4ace (repo unit)** and **Product unit:** what
  `lampOS/deploy/lamp-4ace/` and `lampOS/deploy/product/` set, reading only
  lines that aren't commented out. lamp-4ace may also run drop-ins that aren't
  in the repo (see "What the census found"). A switch that is on by its
  code's default, with no unit setting it, is written exactly `on (unset)`.
  That is the one cell the stable notes read, since no file states that
  default: such a switch is never listed as built but not on.
- **Added by:** the merged pull request that brought the name in, or the commit
  for the ones added to `lamp-voice` directly before pull requests were the rule.
- **Lamp check it named:** what that pull request or its doc said must be seen
  on a lamp, and whether a doc records it **shown**. Where nothing is recorded,
  it says so; no lamp result here is assumed.
- **Proposed product default:** "on (already)" where both units already set it;
  "on" where the evidence is in and it should be switched on; "off until …"
  naming the check that would turn it on; "off" for a diagnostic or a switch
  turned down.
- **Counted as:** `on`, `now`, `until`, `off` or `setting`, for the counts
  (see "The count").
- Paths, sockets and test hooks (`LAMP_*_SOCKET`, `LAMP_*_DIR`, `LAMP_*_FILE`,
  `LAMP_*_TEST_*`, `LAMP_BUS`, `LAMP_BUS_ROOT`, `LAMP_CLOCK`) and tuning numbers
  (speeds, poses, reaches) are left out: they configure, they don't turn a
  behaviour on.

The generic voice template (`deploy/units/lamp-voice.service`) also sets
`LAMP_TOOLS`, `LAMP_CLOCK`, `LAMP_TIME_PLACES`, `LAMP_KERNEL_CLOCK` and
`LAMP_SESSION_FACT` to `on`, matching the product. The update unit census
checks all five, and its function test reads the generic unit to check the
clock declaration, time context and session fact. They need the system
clock and zone files, not product-only configuration. A clock the kernel
does not know to be synchronised is reported as unset; the local zone
still needs to be correct. This is configuration and host-test evidence,
not a moved-lamp spoken-time or latency result.

## Reserved repair controller name

`LAMP_FACE_REPAIR` is reserved by the inactive face-repair controller core.
The core does not read it: setting it in the environment enables no runtime,
RPC or product capability. Its constructor takes an explicit boolean for host
controls. Neither product nor lamp units set it. It is outside the behavior
counts until the separately reviewed runtime integration implements the switch.

## The count

The counts aren't kept on this page: a test works them out from the rows, so
that two pull requests that each add a switch never touch the same line (the
first builder, #705, 18 October 07:28). Each row's last column, **Counted as**,
says how the switch is counted:

| Counted as | Means |
|---|---|
| `on` | On already, or right at its default |
| `now` | Proposed to turn on now |
| `until` | Off until its named lamp check |
| `off` | Stays off (diagnostic, turned down, or not needed) |
| `setting` | A setting, not a behaviour |

To see the counts, by table and in all:

```sh
cargo test -p lamp-voice-eval --test it switches:: -- --nocapture
```

`lamp-voice-eval`'s `tests/it/switches.rs` fails if a row in the four tables
below has no **Counted as**, or one that isn't one of the five. `now` and
`until` are only for a switch the product leaves off: `lampos-promote`'s
notes test fails on one that a product file `lamp-update install` places
turns on, read as the stable notes read the units (any value but off is
on). Its row keeps the check it named, in its own words (Reviewer-two's P3
on #4662). The same tests fail on a row counted `off` that such a file turns on, and on
one counted `on` that none does, unless its **Product unit** is `on (unset)`:
on by its code's default, with nothing set (#4735 found one of each). And a
`LAMP_*` that such a file turns on has a row, unless this page's left-out
list in "How to read it" names it (#4755 added the two that had none).

On 17 October this section proposed `LAMP_LOOK_FULL=on` with `lamp-eyes
--full-look` now, and named as the quickest wins among "off until"
`LAMP_STAFF_NOTE`, `LAMP_SHORT_SOUNDS=quiet-room-checked`,
`LAMP_ASKER_FIRST=record`, `LAMP_STOP_TOLD`, `LAMP_WAIT_KEEPS_ANSWER` and
`LAMP_TASK_NEWS_AT_PAUSE`. All of them are on in the product units since,
and so are `LAMP_TASKS_WHO` and `LAMP_SPOKEN_TO`, so each is counted `on`.
`LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS`, `lamp-eyes --leave-still-faces`, `lamp-eyes
--look-on-change`, `lamp-expression --offline-face` and `lamp-name
--quiet-below` are still off, each until its own check.

| LAMP_ONSET_FADE | `on` only; off; needs `--turn-taking duplex`. From the first block heard over the lamp that could make a yield, the reply handed to the speaker is lowered to -12 dB (`ONSET_FADE_FLOOR`), ramped across each 10 ms chunk, until the yield is decided (`PlaybackMask::fade`); a yield or a commit stops the speaker as before, and a run that comes to nothing brings the reply back over 50 ms (`ONSET_FADE_RISE_CHUNKS`). The canceller's reference is the lowered audio as written | — | — | #5076 (the lead, 5 Nov) | Tests only: `with_the_onset_fade_a_run_over_the_lamp_lowers_the_reply_before_it_yields` on the coordinator's harness | off until a lamp row | A quieter render through the onset can change the echo evidence of the blocks after the first (`Over` or `Own`); a cough or a door lowers the reply for about 0.1 s | until |
| LAMP_CUT_IN_COUNTS_REAL | `on` only; off; needs `--turn-taking duplex`. A resume counts toward the two fruitless yields (`FRUITLESS_YIELDS`) only when its yield's evidence window points at the lamp's own voice: from the onset run through the settling block (at most 120 blocks), `Own` and not-plain `Over` blocks outnumber plain `Over` and `Clear`; `Unknown`, `Under`, `Still` and no evidence count for neither, and a tie does not count (`YieldEvidence`). Any other resume counts on its own bound instead: three (`OTHER_FRUITLESS_YIELDS`) fall back for the reply as two of the lamp's own voice do; the 20 s gives one back to each, a commit clears both, and with `LAMP_CUT_IN_KEEPS_LISTENING` each allows two more. So a sound that is never a line pauses a story at most three times in 20 s (five with keeps-listening). The two counts are separate, not summed: mixed with the lamp's own voice they allow at most four pauses (one own, two others, then one more that falls back whichever count it adds to), eight with keeps-listening (three own, four others, one more). Talk heard in the clear in a yield commits only when its middle level, before cancellation, is no more than 6 dB (`NEAR_ASKER_DB`) under the person the reply answers (`asker_first`'s middle of their own line, of that reply's own turn), judged once 6 blocks of it are heard (`NEAR_ASKER_BLOCKS`). | LAMP_CUT_IN_OVER_BED | `on` only; off; needs `--turn-taking duplex` and `LAMP_STOP_WORDS=heard-always`: with `heard` or without stop words the switch is inert, exactly as off (one journal line at start, which says so). `heard-always` hears "Stop." over every block the lamp speaks and stops the answer as the hand does, yielded or not, so two bed resumes that fall the reply back (no yield until the 20 s forgive one) still leave it heard; with `heard` a stop is heard only in a yield and would go unheard for up to 20 s (the second reader's HOLD on #4872). The cost is `heard-always`'s: each reply's sound goes to the speech-to-text. When the room was busy as the reply began (`busy_lately`, the voice's `own_line_busy`), sound alone commits no yield: neither talk in the clear after it (rule a) nor stillness after plain blocks (rule b); the yield resumes at stillness or its 1.1 s deadline and counts as fruitless, and two such fall the reply back. A stop word, a forced yield (`lamp-name`'s stop), the lamp's name, a head tap or the button still stop the answer. A sound over the answer that never made it yield (no onset block over the lamp) is held 600 ms for the lamp's name or a stop word, then let go as the room's (`over_bed`). `own_line_busy` is taken after the turn the request displaced is counted. In a busy room, stillness also counts only the plain blocks of the run that made the yield, not the lamp's own tail after it (`Interruption::over_bed` in `lamp-audio`); in a quiet room both rules commit exactly as off. "Busy" is three unanswered turns, held 180 s, not sound: a TV that forms no turns is never busy | — | — | #4868 (option 3 with option 1, the lead's agent at the lead's word) | Tests only (`*over_bed*` / `*bed_of_talk*` in lamp-audio and the mask); row-3967d A/B on lamp-52e6, both bands: the product with `LAMP_STOP_WORDS=heard-always` against the same with this switch: a bed alone cuts 0 of 5 answers, "Stop" over the bed stops 4 of 5 by words within 1 s, a quiet "Stop" 3 of 3 | off until those numbers are on #4868 | On lamp-52e6 a TV or two people talking end every answer by rule (a) (out-3967e, 10 of 10), and on lamp-4ace the lamp's own tail ended one by rule (b) (05:41) | until |
| LAMP_BACKCHANNEL_HOLDS | `on` only; off; needs `--turn-taking duplex` and `LAMP_STOP_WORDS=heard` or `heard-always`, inert without either (one journal line says so); with `heard-always` the reply's one stream tells the yield its words, only its own final results, interim ones and those ending before the yield passed over, a backchannel told again as words if a later final adds more (#2939, 23 Oct; reviewer-two's P2 on #3200). While a yield's words are awaited its commit is held for them: on the quiet after a plain word until they come, on speech after the lamp's sound only while that speech is under 700 ms (`BACKCHANNEL_SPEECH_BLOCKS`; 400 ms until lamp-52e6 on 23 Oct, where a played "Yeah." and "Uh-huh." reached it and committed); no hold past 500 ms after the words are asked for (`BACKCHANNEL_WORDS_WAIT_BLOCKS`, after 100 ms of stillness) or past the yield's 1.1 s deadline. Words of one or two, all backchannels (`stop_words::backchannel`, read after hyphens and other dashes are joined: mm, mmm, mhm, mhmm, mmhm, mmhmm, uhhuh, uhhum, yeah, yea, yeh, yah, yes, yep, yup, ok, okay, okey, alright, right, sure; oh, ah, hm and hmm only before another (`LEADS_ONLY`: "Oh, okay." is one, a lone "Oh." or "Hmm." commits); a pair "I see", "all right", "uh huh", "uh hum", "mm hm", "mm hmm" or "got it" counting as one word; uh, uh-uh, so, no, what and huh left out; Vietnamese with `stt_language` vi: ừ, ừm, ờ, à, ồ, dạ, vâng, ok; not "rồi" or "đúng"), no "?", no stop phrase, and the lamp's last finished sentence before the yield not a question (a "Yes." to "Shall I go on?" is an answer), let the resume wait resume the answer and no turn starts; a backchannel commits only once its speech reaches 700 ms. Anything else commits at once; words that come empty or only punctuation ("...") are no words; no words by the bound, or before the yield ends, resume the answer as a backchannel does (room sound or the lamp's own echo, lamp-52e6 on #3200), and the journal says the hold ran out; only speech reaching 700 ms commits without words, or speech going on 60 ms in the clear after the words were asked for (the ask heard only what came before its pause: lamp-52e6, 24 Oct, "Wait", a 300 ms pause, then a question, came back as no words and was held to the deadline); words that come later are dropped with the yield. A stop phrase, "wait" and a forced `stop/heard` are never held. A backchannel resume counts as neither fruitless nor other-fruitless. `duplex_commit` and `duplex_resume` record `held_ms` and `backchannel` (null: no words) | — | — | #2939 (design on #2602, the first builder's yes 22 Oct) | Tests only. lamp-52e6 at 09:44 on 23 Oct with `heard-always` and the switch unset: 5 of 5 backchannels committed and cut the story. Lamp check owed on lamp-52e6 with `LAMP_STOP_WORDS=heard` or `heard-always`: five backchannels resume with no resume over a person's talk, and "Uh", "So", "Ahem" and the stop words commit; then a person's own mm-hm | off: an A/B said no. lamp-4ace, 31 Oct, `c08b4c08f6`, the product unit against it plus `LAMP_STOP_WORDS=heard` and this switch, P, W, P, W, 6 real cut-ins and 4 tries of the lamp's own tail an arm: on handled 1 of 6 real cut-ins against 4 of 6 off and yielded later (median 1140 against 590 ms); neither arm committed on its own tail, so its fix had nothing to show. "Neither switch goes on" ([the first builder on #4710, 09:10Z](https://github.com/autonomous-ai/lampOS/issues/4710#issuecomment-6541531293); Sixth, [09:11Z](https://github.com/autonomous-ai/lampOS/issues/4710#issuecomment-6541543896)) | A person's "Mm-hm." while the lamp spoke committed as a new turn and lost the answer (9 of 12 short cut-ins on 22 Oct) | off |
| LAMP_CUT_IN_LATE_YIELD | `on` only; off; needs `--turn-taking duplex`. A yield made late in a person's speech over the lamp commits on stillness instead of resuming the answer: at least 14 blocks heard plainly as a person over the lamp (`LATE_YIELD_OVER_BLOCKS`; plain: the render explains at most 20% of the block), onset run included, in the 150 blocks up to and including the one that made the yield (`LATE_YIELD_WINDOW_BLOCKS`). Without it such a yield finds nothing to commit: nothing is heard in the clear for 30 blocks after it (`clear_after_blocks`) while the lamp's sound dies away, the person has finished by then, and 40 still blocks (`silence_blocks`) resume the answer. In a busy room: a face turned to the lamp is never a reason on its own (`attention::Attention::face_shows`: the rule's `TURNED`, the short sound's, the journal's sign, `--spoken-to`'s check, the remark and Policy arms, and the second route's call that carries the next lines), though it still confirms the name heard in the sound (the strict busy form, a short sound, `LAMP_NAME_SECOND_SIGN`); and a line is in the conversation going on when it began while the lamp's own words played or within 8 s of their end, however long it then goes on, and nobody has talked past the lamp since (`name_or_follow_up::FOLLOW_UP_FOR`; the second route's call carries 8 s too), in place of the thread's 45 s and a busy room's 15 s read as the line is judged (lamp-52e6, 22 Oct: follow-ups begun as the answers played, 0 of 3 answered); later, the name, a tap or its words calling the lamp let a line out. In a room not known to be busy, as without the switch: a face is a reason and the windows are as they were (the owner, 22 Oct 18:0x: a person alone with the lamp does not say its name). So the room turns busy from the talk the lamp holds: there a line held as said to someone else by name (`attention::said_to_another`), or as said to nobody with someone in view and turned away (`bus_out::someone_in_view_turned_away`), is talk even with `LAMP_SPOKEN_TO`, never a lone voice's held lines with nobody in view (lamp-4ace, 22 Oct: an unseen person's misheard call made the room busy and the scene was lost) (`room_told::RoomTold::count_held_talk`), and two faces in the eyes' newest fresh look (`bus_out::faces_in_view`) (with `LAMP_TWO_FACES_HELD`, only two faces that stay) or a line in another voice (`LAMP_SAME_VOICE_MODEL`) make it busy at once (`RoomTold::two_people`). Said close to it, a reply to its news and the privacy and Policy holds are as before. What the model is told of the conversation is unchanged | — | — | The owner's desk test on lamp-4ace (22 Oct); the quiet room as before, the owner at 18:0x on #2967 | Tests only: in a busy room a face alone held, played with the name heard, a follow-up begun 5 s after the lamp's words played however late its answer and one begun 12 s after without the name held, on both routes; in a quiet room a face and a follow-up 15 s after played; two held lines and two faces make a quiet room busy. On lamp-4ace from 17:06 on 22 Oct (the busy-room rule everywhere): desk talk held, the name and follow-ups answered, the name needed too often alone | off until a real desk-talk set passes, with one person alone answered without the name | At the owner's desk on 22 Oct the owner talked to a colleague and the lamp answered 18 times: 8 "someone is turned to the lamp", 6 "a conversation with the lamp is going on", 4 its name. Known costs: in a busy room an answer to the lamp's own question begun more than the follow-up window (8 s unless `LAMP_FOLLOW_UP_FOR_MS` sets it) after its words needs the name; in a room not known to be busy, a person alone facing the lamp on a phone call is answered line after line, as before the switch: the lamp holds none of it, and one face makes no busy room; a colleague off camera, with no voice model, is answered while the person faces the lamp, until a line is held; a face on a screen or in a photo keeps the room busy, so the name is needed | until |

The privacy slide is the lamp's sleep and wake (migrate-P4, #5182): closing it asks the sleep a hand's hold asks, and a slide that has stayed open for 1 s (`slide_wake::SETTLE_US`) asks the wake a tap asks, so it rises, stretches, says "hm?" and listens with no touch. Closing turns the restart guard's mute into that sleep, and opening clears it either way; a new fault arms the guard again. A mute chosen on the POWER button stays, and the onboarding walk never sleeps. A slide first read open, or read again after it could not be read, asks nothing. | LAMP_SECOND_NAME_CARRIES | `on`; off. On the second route, a line that is only the lamp's name ("Lamp.", "Hey Lamp", any case or punctuation; `name_alone`), a short one begun with it (the name set off first, at most 3 words after it, not whole as it is: no stop or "never mind", no question, no sentence ended over two words past the name; "Lamp. So", "Hey Lamp, what's" wait, "Lamp, stop." and "Lamp, lights off." don't; `name_first`), or that the coordinator ended as only the name (`Called`, its words perhaps a name misheard, "Life."), is never held as a short sound, and its answer waits up to 2.5 s after its end (`NAME_CARRIES_FOR`). A line begun in that wait, in the same session and privacy generation and not after a stop, drops the name's request and, only when one person said both by the join's judgement (one face seen speaking each, prints at 0.70 or more, or no sign of a second person in a quiet room, never an unknown or busy one; `Heeding::one_speaker`), is said by the name: the rule judges it with the name line's words before its own ("Lamp, " for a name misheard) and its window from the name's first sound, so the name heard there counts and earns the name note as a heard name does, and it is asked with those words. Otherwise the line is judged as its own and the name answered in its place if it is held, never joined to it, with a note that someone went on talking right after the name, never what was said (`worker::NAME_THEN_HELD`), so the lamp says it didn't catch it and asks them to say it again, rather than only "Mm? I'm here." (lamp-4ace, 26 Oct, the first line after a start in a room not yet known to be quiet). The caller's face seen speaking turned away is held and ends the carry, their voice sure or not; turning back needs a fresh call or answer. These signs are read in any room: in a room not busy, unknown or quiet, such a line, or one in a voice not surely the owner's, is held as in a busy room, the room unchanged. The owner's carried line is no reply to a colleague's question held before it (no `AFTER_QUESTION` hold). Voice-only identity carry must be nearer the conversation's than every held line's print by `CARRY_NEARER_BY` (0.10, provisional): up to eight per-call request/optional-print constraints, no eviction. Duplicates only fill missing evidence; owner admissions/answers do not reset constraints, but a fresh call, stop, privacy, observed forget, turn-away or two consecutive other lines clears them and fences old callbacks. In short, the first 8 s are not identity-gated; past 8 s only a sure same-speaker sign carries; always held, inside the 8 s too: another face seen speaking, the caller turned away, and a sure other voice (not reachable today: prints carry at most 1.5 s of speech, under the provisional 2.5 s). A print that was no evidence never becomes evidence: a going-on line's print joins the conversation's voice only at 0.70 or over or seen spoken by the caller's face, at its verdict or late, caller-only on or off. Each answer renews the 20 s, so a chain of turns has no overall cap, but outside the 8 s window each carried turn needs its own sure sign. A keep in a line admitted as the room's may go to the known face the eyes name by its end plus 300 ms (#2807, memory.md): with the eyes' view (`LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING=on` and `LAMP_BUS=on`, whose `known-face` shows it), the face must be in view at the line's start, whether its face cache was cold as it started or went cold only during it (the eyes' known file changed mid-line, as on lamp-c0a4 on 23 Oct 05:13); without the eyes' view, a line begun while the cache is cold is kept to a person only if they're named within one refresh (200 ms) of its start, and one gone cold only during it keeps for nobody | on |

| LAMP_PERSON_SCOPE_SEEN | `on` exactly; off. The second route only, with `LAMP_SECOND_SPEAKING_FACE`: a line is in a known person's scope only when their own face alone was seen speaking it (`speaking_faces::not_theirs`, `seen_saying`), and when no face was seen speaking it but theirs was the only one in view through it (at least 3 looks, read speaking in at least one, still in under half: `SpeakingFaces::alone_in_view`), it stays the room's and only its reply is told their name (`ScopeLease::named_for_reply`; lamp-3e83 on 28 Oct, the owner alone at the lamp lost their name; reviewer-three's P2 and the Reviewer-lead's P1 on #4090), each id and the known face read with the eyes' boot (`speakers_with_boot`, `known_with_boot`), so a reused id after the eyes restart is not theirs (Second's P1 on #3437), and with no eyes' reader (the bus would not open) no face is seen, so every line and late keeper is the room's (reviewer-two's P2 on #3437), and with `LAMP_SECOND_PREPARED` a person-scoped line is not prepared on its early words, so nothing is asked for them before its end is read (Second's P2 on #3437); else it is told, its memory calls run and its exchange filed and observed as the room's. Off, only another face seen speaking it narrows it, so no face seen speaking leaves it the person's. The same rule for a line's own admission and for a late look's keeper (`ScopeLease::keeper_within`); one way only, a narrowed lease never widens (`ScopeLease::late_open`). So with the owner's face in view and silent, an off-camera "forget everything about me" runs as the room's, where `forget_everything` refuses, and an off-camera "I'm flying to Tokyo" is not kept or observed as theirs | — | on | reviewer-three's P1 and P2 on #3435 (09:19), Second's late-keeper case (09:24), Sixth's plan on #3126 | Tests only (`second_provider/tests/person_scope_seen.rs`: the off-camera forget and line with the owner silent in view, the cold-cache late keeper with the owner silent, the owner seen speaking keeps their scope, a reused id after the eyes restart or no eyes' reader refuses the scope and the keeper, switch off as before; `speaking_faces` for the rule) | on: with the memory switches (25 Oct), so a line no face was seen speaking is the room's (#3437, reviewer-three's P1 and P2 on #3435) | Known cost: an owner whose speaking read misses (fewer than 3 looks, a turned or covered mouth) gets the room's scope (alone in view, its reply is still told their name, so a partner off camera while the owner nods may be answered by the owner's name, nothing kept or forgotten for them) for that line, so a personal keep or forget is not done as theirs: `forget_everything` refuses, and a keep is the room's, or withheld with `LAMP_MEMORY_PERSONAL_NEEDS_FACE`; with `LAMP_SECOND_PREPARED` also on, the owner's own lines lose the early start too, even when their face is seen speaking (reviewer-three's P3 on #3437) | on |

| LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIED_SAVES | `on` exactly; off. With `LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIES`: a carried line's `remember` (`ScopeLease::save_carried`) is held in RAM, never written or journalled, for the carried person's plain yes on the next line within 60 s, their own face seen saying it (`LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING`, as a held forget's yes); the lamp asks "Want me to keep that for you?". The yes may say the keep back ("Yes, please keep that.", "Keep it.", or "keep off" beside a yes, as speech to text heard "keep that" on lamp-06c1, 5 Nov, S14; `known_faces::plain_agreement_to_keep`); an answer that is neither a yes nor a no (`known_faces::unclear_answer_to_keep`) is asked about once more, "Should I keep that?", running nothing. Anything else (no yes, a yes not seen, another face, a forget, the conversation's end) drops it. The carried line's exchange is never journalled. Its scope stays held: no recall, note or hits, no forget, `forget_everything`, rule, face or yes; a line admitted held only by a face's gap (`LAMP_FACE_GAP_HOLD`) holds nothing; a narrowed line never | — | on | #4339 (the memory's owner, 30 Oct: "We booked the Pine Hill Hotel" on a carried line got "I can't save that just now"); the first builder's call on #4508 (writes held for the face) | Tests only (`second_provider/tests/person_scope_seen.rs`, `ScopeLease::save_carried`); a played check with the face slipping mid-talk owed | on: a carried line's keep is asked about, and kept on their seen yes | Privacy-rule change, approved 30 Oct: their own seen yes confirms a keep whose words someone off camera may have said; a same-voice check is a follow-up | on |
| LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIED_MEMORY | `on` exactly; off. With `LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIES`: a line carried in full as a known person's conversation (`ScopeLease::carried_for`; never one carried for a keep only, `carry_keep_only`, nor one admitted held only by a face's gap) is read and kept as theirs, as a lone known face's line is with `LAMP_PERSON_SCOPE_ONE_FACE_READS` (`ScopeLease::read_carried`): its scope is theirs, not held, so recall, hits, the memory note and their thread read their memory, and its `remember` is written in their scope at once (`ScopeLease::kept_by_one_face`, `remember::run_one_face_keep`), not held for a yes (it wins over `LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIED_SAVES`). No forget of any kind (one thing, by what or which, bare, everything, a face), no correction (`replaces`), no rule kept, listed or forgotten and no yes runs on it (`ScopeLease::forgets_need_mouth`, `second_provider::one_face_refused`, the memory route in `tools.rs`); it holds no forget for a yes (`forget_asker`). Its exchange is still never journalled, summarised or put in a talk note (`ScopeLease::reads_carried`, `worker::keep_scoped`). Who counts as carried is unchanged. Needs all of: the one-face rule's tracker (`LAMP_PERSON_SCOPE_ONE_FACE` on and acting) showing no other voice in the conversation, nor, with `LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIES_PAST_SILENT`, its sign of another person (`second_provider::no_other_voice_shown`, fails closed); no voice print for the rules needed since 10 Nov (the memory owner's call: with `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_FOR=services` another voice is told apart only by the rule's other signs and the faces, `Engine::reading_carried_where_heard`, said once at start); the rule judging the line's own voice (a line it skipped is narrowed to the room's, but a line with no words of its own answering, in its place, words cut short that were settled as the person it is carried for keeps their scope: `memory_scope::mark_said_line`, `second_provider::said_saying`, lamp-0c4e, 10 Nov); and the person's own face the one face in view through the line by the one-face count (`SpeakingFaces::one_face_in_view`), not only seen in the 5 s before. Without any of them (no tracker, no voice check, their face gone) a carried line stays as before, held and the room's. Any later narrow (another voice heard as the line is judged, `LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIES_PAST_SILENT`'s second look) makes it the room's, keeps and all. Off: a carried line as before, byte for byte | — | on | Friday, 9 Nov (the memory owner's decision "do both 1 and 2 now", after lamp-06c1's S14 run on build 10824: every carried line refused its recall, "no face was seen speaking it", so 11 of 14 asks failed though the facts were stored) | Tests only (`second_provider/tests/person_scope_seen.rs` `with_carried_memory_*`, `memory_scope.rs`); lamp-06c1's S14 rerun with the switch on owed: carried asks find the person's facts, a carried forget is refused | on in the product: the memory owner's call on 10 Nov, with no played row first, after lamp-3e83 answered its known person from the room's memory | Known risk, as for `LAMP_PERSON_SCOPE_ONE_FACE_READS`: a guest off camera within 5 s of the person's face, their face still alone in view and silent, no other face in view and the same-voice check hearing no other voice, is told the person's recall and may add a keep to their memory; nothing is ever forgotten, corrected or agreed on such a line | on |

A line that rule takes (their known face the one face in view, none seen speaking it, not carried, no other voice heard) is not narrowed: it is told and read as theirs (`ScopeLease::read_by_one_face`: recall, hits, the memory note, their thread and its carry), and its exchange is journalled in their record by both journal backends (`memory_scope::one_face_record`, `remember::filed_by_one_face`); a late-named one keeps its reads the room's but its record is theirs (`ScopeLease::keep_for_one_face_recorded`). No forget of any kind and no yes runs on such a line (`ScopeLease::forgets_need_mouth`, `second_provider::one_face_refused`, the memory route in `tools.rs`), the single-item `forget` the keep's rule reaches (`reached_by_one_face`) among them, and it holds no forget for a yes (`forget_asker`). Nor anything beyond saving and recall: a `remember` correcting a kept thing (`replaces`, `remember::corrects`; the implicit correction keeps plainly), `keep_rule`, `list_rules`, `set_role` and `end_role` (`LAMP_MEMORY_ROLE`, also refused in `remember::role_for_guarded`; the privacy read on #5385), `cancel_reminder` and `snooze_reminder` (these two run with `LAMP_ONE_FACE_REMINDERS` on, the 4 Nov acceptance) are refused, the observation pass of a conversation such an exchange was filed in retires nothing (with `LAMP_MEMORY_REPLACES`, no `"replaces"`: `Conversation::filed`, `remember::job_replacing`), and the line has no services owner (`services::owner_of`, so no `service_read`) unless the private room gives it one. A second face, an unknown face, no face, another voice or a carried line: as before. Off: the one-face rule as before, byte for byte | — | on | Friday, 6 Nov (#5522; the memory owner's 16:43 "apply it" and 16:49 on #5385) | A played row owed (the owner's call: tests now, the lamp later): a lone known face never read speaking keeps, recalls and is journalled as theirs; a forget it says is refused; a second face or voice is the room's | on in the product: the memory owner's call on 10 Nov, with no played row first, after lamp-3e83 answered its known person from the room's memory | lamp-220c and 29e7: the face alone in 17 looks, read speaking in 0, so their words and recall went to the room. Known risk, accepted: a guest off camera, the person's face silent and alone in view and no other voice heard, is told the person's recall, and the guest's exchange is filed in the person's record, so their day recall (and a nightly extraction, if on) may take the guest's words as theirs; `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_CONFIRMS` tells the guest apart only once the conversation has a print. | LAMP_HISTORY_KEEPS_CALLS | `on` only; off unless set; the product's `lamp-voice.service` sets it (on in the product since 3 Nov). On the second route, the functions a turn ran that answered with no error and no keep refused are kept with its exchange in the thread: one made with no arguments (the light's and the volume's steps, on and off, mute), and a light's or volume's call (`light_*`, `volume_*` but the app's two, as `lamp_actions::is_short`) or a Schedule's (`set_timer`, `set_reminder`, `repeat_reminder`, `list_reminders`, `cancel_reminder`, `snooze_reminder`, `tools::SCHEDULE_NAMES`) with its own arguments, `light_colour {"colour":"red"}`, `set_timer {"text":"Timer","seconds":60}`, at most 256 bytes of compact JSON, past which it is kept bare if its function takes none, else not kept (#5045(a), `second_provider::kept_args`; the Schedule's since lamp-52e6 at 9928 on 4 Nov: "Lamp, set a timer to one minute." made no call and said "Timer set. It rings at 9:39 am.", copying the earlier reply, and no timer existed); a service write (`service_write`) with only `service`, `action` and `send_id` (`WRITE_KEPT_FIELDS`: never a recipient, subject, body, title, event id or `said`), its result told as its status, prepared, done or not done (`kept_result`, `WRITE_PREPARED`/`WRITE_DONE`/`WRITE_NOT_DONE`), not "(done earlier; its result was not kept)" (lamp-06c1 at 11913, 11 Nov: with earlier writes told as words alone, the second create, move and mail of a conversation were read back by the model itself, no call); the arguments stay in the thread in RAM and are never journalled; never another call that took arguments (a keep, a skill's), which a kept call's `{}` would teach calling bare, nor `more_functions`, a decline, a held or refused call, or a reminder's or an add-on's quoted line's. A later turn's history tells that exchange with its calls before the lamp's words: an assistant message with the calls (ids `kept-<request>-<n>`, each with the arguments it was kept with), a `tool` message for each, "(done earlier; its result was not kept)", then the words (`worker::messages_marked`, `Thread::called`). The calls go with the exchange's own scope; a forget drops them; not written to the thread file (#1505), so after a restart earlier exchanges are words only. At most 8 calls an exchange, each name with the same arguments once and up to 64 bytes. A chat that refuses round 1 (a status that isn't 429 or 5xx) with kept calls in the history is asked once more without them, in words only (`without_kept_calls`, the Reviewer-lead's P2 on #5018), so a refused history never ends a turn "can't answer". A skill added from a file in the app: the app uploads a package or a bare `SKILL.md` to the backend, which sends `skills.upload.<upload_id>` with no file; `lamp-cloud` answers `starting`, fetches `GET <base>/devices/get-message?kind=skills.upload.<upload_id>` with its key, whose data carries the file (`{upload_id, name, version, format, sha256, size, content_base64}`), decodes it (at most 1 MiB), checks its size, SHA-256 and id, refuses a Store-signed package ("add it from the Skill Store"), `run.py` or any other file, and a name on the lamp, then hands the two files to `lamp-voice`'s app-make socket (`{"upload": …}`), which holds them to a made skill's cap with no trigger and a function, says them by `plainly`, checks and packs them, keeps them (at most five made or added), puts them on at once with no spoken yes (#5977), and says aloud "I added NAME from a file, not from the Skills Store, and it's on: WHAT IT DOES, using WHAT IT USES.". `success` once it is on, or `failure` on a check or when it couldn't be put on, follows as the kind's second reply, so the backend deletes the file. The person writes a skill in the app in their own words: a name, when to use it and what to do (at most 40, 200 and 2,000 bytes, plain text). `lamp-voice`'s app-make socket (it listens with this switch alone too) takes `{"written": {"name", "when", "instructions", "request_id"}}` and writes the package itself, no model: a function for the model with no trigger and only `speak`, whose call's answer is the person's instructions, framed as theirs, word for word; checked by `lamp-skills pack` and kept as a made skill (`made_skill::keep_written`). A name any skill on the lamp has (installed or pushed) is refused; a kept one of that name not on is replaced by the new one, so a fix goes at once. Kept, it goes on at once with no spoken yes (#5977), and the lamp says "I made NAME, and it's on: WHAT IT DOES, using WHAT IT USES." or why it couldn't. `can` names `skills.write` and `skills.made` on the switch alone | — | — | the first builder's yes on the Queue, 3 Nov 14:33 | A skill written in the app on a lamp: kept, asked aloud, put on by a spoken yes, and then called by the model when the person asks for it, its answer followed | off until its lamp check | Text the person wrote reaches the model as instructions; the model's own rules still hold, and it goes on with no spoken yes, the app's ask being the ask | until |
| LAMP_MAKE_SKILL_AGENT | `on` only; off. Read by the second route's worker (passed by `process::second_route_worker_environment`), with `LAMP_MAKE_SKILL=on` and a tasks part (`--tasks-dir`). A voice make the lamp's own model didn't make goes to the lamp's own agent, OpenClaw or Hermes (the tasks part's `lamp` target), when the lamp has one (`crate::make_skill_agent`). The order: DeepSeek writes first; with an agent on the lamp it gets one try and no repairs, and an answer not kept (the tidies, `lamp-skills pack`, the say-back, a `cannot:` of its own other than the brief's own refusals, `made_skill::BRIEFS_OWN_REFUSALS`, which are said as they are, or no answer) is handed at once to the agent, with the same system prompt and request (in a file under `/run/lampos-make-skill` that the task names, 0640 in the `lamp-agents` group, the person's own addresses as placeholders, removed once answered: the tasks part takes 2,000 bytes), prepared and confirmed at once on the voice's own socket. The agent's answer goes through the same checks and gets up to 3 repairs of its own, each answer within 45 s and the agent's part within 120 s. Once its hand-over is taken the lamp says "Let me think about that a little longer." through the say service, never after a cut turn. The agent's own `cannot:` is said as "I couldn't make that one.". No agent on the lamp: DeepSeek keeps its 3 repairs, as today. No answer from the agent in 45 s: its task is sent `cancel` (the tasks part has no op that ends work its agent has) and DeepSeek's one answer, given no repairs, stands as not made. The journal says "made by DeepSeek", "DeepSeek failed: handed to the agent" and "made by the agent". The app's typed makes are unchanged | — | — | #6059's proposal (the owner's OK, 11 Nov; the first builder's yes, 22:44) | The 16 makes (twelve simple, four known DeepSeek failures) on a lamp with an agent, comparing kept, the say-back and the time with DeepSeek's | off until its lamp check | A make DeepSeek can't make gets a second writer, the lamp's own agent, before it is said as not made | until |

With `LAMP_MEMORY_KEEP_HEARD`, a correction keeps the model's text and its `replaces` when each of its key words is the old fact's (as this session was shown it) or was heard, compared without accents; otherwise the heard line is kept instead, without `replaces`: the old one stays and the heard line is kept beside it, and the answer says `not_replaced` with the old id. Needs a lamp-memory from the same build: an older memory refuses the new fields (`deny_unknown_fields`), so every remember with `replaces` is answered `bad_request` until both are updated. Every outcome of a `service_read` or `service_write` the owner asked writes one service record to the Days memory (`service_record`, `service_records`, `service_records_forget`; `service_records` table), kept until forgotten: `prepared` at the read-back, then `done` (with the service's id), `cancelled` (each waiting-write drop, with why, such as the lamp answering after the read-back with no send), `refused` (the gate's reason), `failed` (the service's error, or no answer in time: not known whether it went) or `timed_out` (two minutes with no send); a read is `done` with its query and, of what it found, titles, senders, dates, links and a short excerpt only past the services' security check, or `no_result`. The owner's writes are saved in full. The #5853 send receipts live in the same ledger (`service_records::keep_send`), so a send's answer sets its receipt and its record once, and a cut-in turn reads the same receipt. Saved beside the turn on a bounded queue (32) by a thread of its own; memory down, it waits and is retried, said once in the journal; one journal line a change, key, service, action and status only. Forget everything and forget about a thing remove them, with the switch on or off (records kept while it was on are never left), never undoing anything at the service. Off, nothing is kept or written and the receipts are kept as before. | LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_NOT_ASKER | `on` only, with Days memory; off; reaches both voice routes. A session's note says the room's kept things, and the earlier exchanges, were said by whoever was in the room, never necessarily the one asking now; a room-kept fact naming a person or saying "I'm ..." is told as "Someone in the room said: ...". A known person's own snapshot keeps its facts, and is said to be theirs, to be told as theirs, never as someone here's (#3504, N9). Counts only in the journal | — | on (26 Oct) | #2176 | lamp-4ace: `open-name.sh` / `names.sh` "new session" with a room-kept played name ("Dylan"): "What's my name?" never answered with it as the asker's | on: lamp-4ace, 26 Oct, lamp-voice 2a8273ef2's product unit, played: a fresh session's "how do I take my coffee?" after a room's "I take my coffee black" answered as the asker's in 3 of 3 rounds without it, in 0 of 2 with it; names.sh gave no room name back either way | The "Dylan" case, 21 Oct 03:11-03:14: the room's kept name was given as the asker's in 2 of 3 rounds (memory.md) | on |
| LAMP_MEMORY_PERSONAL_NEEDS_FACE | `on` only; off; reaches both voice routes. While nobody is known (the room's scope), a `remember` whose text LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_AFTER_FORGET's detector (`remember::about_someone`: first or third person words, "the user", or a possessive `'s`) marks as about a person is not kept, and the model is told `{"remembered": false}` with a note to say, short and friendly, that it wasn't kept and that they can say who they are or let the lamp see their face and say it again (no promise of a later keep); the observation pass drops such proposals. A room fact is kept, and a known face's own is kept to them. An unknown speaker's single-item forget runs on the room (older room observations may contain a guest's words); its result says what room memory was removed or that nothing matched, never claiming a personal erase. The second voice route waits up to 300 ms for a late known face before choosing that person or the room. Forget everything about a person still requires a known face. Typed app text is always room-scoped: it parses its own text for a named forget and has no spoken `heard_for` lookup. Counts only in the journal | — | on (the lead's decision 1, 23 Oct; since #3174, pinned by install.rs) | the owner's question 1, option (b), on #2849's LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_AFTER_FORGET | lamp-c0a4: an unknown speaker's "my favourite colour is blue" is not kept and the answer promises nothing; "the plant needs water on Fridays" is kept | on | Twelve's c0a4 find: "the user's favourite colour is blue" said with no face known was kept to the room | on |
| LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_SPEAKER | `on` only, with Days memory; off; reaches the second route only, the one that reads which face was seen saying each line. A room line said by one known person keeps their id beside it (`speaker` on the room's `append_linked` and `remember`, the memory's `speakers` table), only when the eyes saw their face, and no other, saying it (`seen_saying`): never a face only in view, two faces, or the voice alone; it needs `LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING=on`, and without it the start says so and no line has a speaker. With `LAMP_MEMORY_FORGET_ALL`, their "forget everything" also sends `forget_said`: the room's lines they said go (not a version someone else said), and the observations made only from them; a kept thing goes only by its own speaker, and a thing also made from someone else's words stays. The id is never told nor picks what is said. An older memory: the line is sent again without it and kept, once journalled, and `forget_said`'s `unknown_op` removes nothing more. A line that plainly asks the lamp to keep something at a sentence's start (`remember::asked::asks_to_keep`: "remember that …", "remember my …", "please remember …", "can you remember that …", "don't forget that …", "keep in mind that …", "make a note that …") must call `remember` in its first chat round (`tool_choice`), after a named ask and a face ask, which come first. Never a question of what the lamp knows ("do you remember …?", "remember the time …?"), a reminder ("remember to …"), a face or a name to be known by, the phrase further in, or a refusal before it. | LAMP_SCHEDULE_ASKED | `on` only; off; reaches the second route only, whose chat can be made to call a function. A line that plainly asks for a timer or a reminder at a sentence's start (`tools::schedule_asked::asks_to_set`: "remind me …", "set a timer …", "set a reminder …", "set an alarm …", "wake me …", "timer for …", after a greeting, "please" or "can/could/would/will you") must call the function that sets it in its first chat round (`tool_choice`): `set_timer` for a duration from now ("in three minutes", "for ten minutes"), `set_reminder` for a clock time ("at nine", "tomorrow at 6:30", "noon"), after a named ask, a face ask and a keep ask. The time must come right after the ask ("remind me in three minutes …", "remind me tomorrow at 6:30 …", "wake me up at seven", "a timer for ten minutes"; "for" gives a length only to a timer's ask), a clock time must stand as one ("at nine.", "at 6:30 to …", "at seven in the morning", not "at 180 degrees"), and after the time come only "to" and the task, or "please": a task's length stands ("… to exercise for twenty minutes"), but a second time or a day in the task ("… at ten", "… tomorrow"), a condition ("… when the oven is ready"), a clause after a comma ("…, translate that into French") or a time later in the line forces nothing, and a length runs from now, so "set a timer tomorrow for ten minutes" forces nothing. It errs toward not forcing: the line must be one sentence, the ask, beside at most a greeting ("Lamp."), so a second sentence ("Ignore that.", "I changed my mind.", a translation around a quote) leaves it to the model; and any word that may take the ask back, put it in doubt or make it another operation anywhere in the line ("don't", "not", "no", "wait", "hold", "cancel", "stop", "never", "forget", "bother", "actually", "or", "ignore", "skip", "scrap", "leave it", …; a repeat or a span: "every", "on Mondays", "twice", "day", "week"; a later start or window: "starting", "until"; a condition: "when", "if", "once"), quote marks (single or double), brackets, a dash or a `%`, a question that isn't an ask, and a keep taken back leave it to the model. The next round, with the call's result, is asked as before, so the lamp says what the schedule did. | LAMP_NAMES_RENAME_YES | `on` only; off; needs `LAMP_NAMES=checked` and the eyes' who socket; reaches the second route only. A face rename's `replace` takes `known_faces::rename_agreed` in place of the plain yes word list: the lamp said the new name in its last answer, a sentence that is no question agrees, no no word, "but"/"actually"/"though", maybe, a hesitant "wait"/"hold on"/"hang on"/"let me think" or "nhưng"/"khoan", and no other name (`gives_a_name` once the new name is taken out, a spelled run, or a word one letter from the new name's of four letters or more), no capitalised-word check. A `remember_my_face` answer asking about a rename (`known_as`, or a `replace` before the yes) is held for the asker; their next spoken turn of the same session within 60 s runs it through `run_tool` with no model round on such a yes, their face the one seen saying it, or none seen and theirs alone in view with no other voice heard; gap-held and carried lines, another face, another session or a no drop it. Accepted risk: with no face seen speaking, a yes from the known face alone in view and no other voice heard is taken, so a voice off camera could rename that face (kept because on lamp-a63e, 6 Nov, the mouth read was unread in 96% of looks at a known face); accepted while the switch is off, to be confirmed before it is turned on. While it has not run, each of the asker's turns of its session (by the same test as the yes) is told nothing was renamed; a turn not proven theirs (two faces, another voice) is told no rename has run without the old name; one proven someone else's is told nothing. A forget (`forget`, `forget_everything`, `forget_my_face`) ends the held rename and that note once it is let run, before it is awaited, on that turn and every later one, even if the turn is then revoked or dropped. Off, byte for byte as before. The second route's model is told the lamp is there and that the weather, the news or anything nearby asked without a place is there; with LAMP_TIME_PLACES the time is said as that place's, not the zone's city. With none, the model is told it doesn't know where it is beyond its time zone. Set or not, it is told it doesn't know which part of town the person is in: for something near them, or a place it isn't sure it heard right, it asks which area before it searches and never picks one, says where it looked when they named no place, and never asks which area for the weather or the time (`local_time::place_told`, `AREA_ASKED`, #4337). The typed name leaves the model's instruction and is told with a line only when it is the owner's: the line's memory person (its scope lease, narrowed to the room once another face is seen speaking) is the known face the eyes keep under the typed name, with at most that one face in view (`humans/faces/tracked`). Nobody in view (a quiet room too: a visitor the camera can't see), an unknown face, another known face, two faces, or no look: never. A lone known face kept under another name is counted in the journal, without a name. The second route judges every turn on that line's own lease, with or without `LAMP_SECOND_SCOPED_CONTEXT`, never on the person its session is counted for (that stays the owner's for 30 s after they go), and tells it right after the character; a turn with no lease is told none. The Live route tells it as a note on the open line, counted told only once the service answers that line (a line given up before its End never wrote it, so the next is told it); a session that may hold it is renewed fresh, between turns, as soon as whoever is at the lamp may not have it, and a line that begins in it before then is not sent (`crates/voice/src/person.rs`, `may_tell`, `live_holds`). Without a memory there is no person lease, so the Live route never tells it. Just after setup no face is kept, so the owner isn't told their name until "remember my face" under the typed name. As memory's scope today: a photo or video of the owner reads as the owner (no liveness check), and the owner held through `LAMP_FACE_GAP_HOLD` (up to about 12 s) can pass the name to someone stepping in out of view | — | on (#4461, beside `LAMP_PERSON_NAME_BY_DEFAULT`) | #2602 (Friday's probe on #3014: the typed name put to unknown faces 60/80) | The owner's face, a visitor's and nobody in view, each asked "what's my name?": the owner's name for the owner only; then off, as before | on (#4461): with `LAMP_PERSON_NAME_BY_DEFAULT`, the name is told only by `may_tell` or by default, never in the character | A visitor called by the owner's name is wrong; off by default since the product sets LAMP_PERSON_NAME and this changes what it tells | on |
| LAMP_PERSON_NAME_BY_DEFAULT | `on` only; the typed name (LAMP_PERSON_NAME), else on a lamp set up by voice the face that ended who_you_are, kept as the owner, while the eyes still know them; off; the second route only (passed to the second worker) | — | on | #3884, the first builder's call (28 Oct 20:14) after lamp-29e7 (8250) and lamp-3e83: the owner alone at their desk lamp asked "what's my name?" right after setup and wasn't answered, the camera reading their mouth speaking on 2 of 18 lines | The owner alone at 52e6, looking away, asks "what's my name?" and is answered by name; with a second voice in the conversation, or a stranger's face in view, they aren't | on | Each reply is told the person speaking is most likely the one who set the lamp up (`person::by_default`), unless someone else is there now (`person::someone_else_now`): another voice in the conversation (the second route's own sign), a face not at the desk read speaking in the last 10 s (`LAMP_FACES_AT_DESK_ONLY`), more than one face in at least two of the eyes' looks of the last 3 s (`known_faces::most_faces_lately`, so one look that missed a face never makes two people one, nor one that saw a face that isn't there one person two; lamp-52e6, 29 Oct), one face the eyes don't know as the owner (their known file read while at most 8 s old, `known_faces::known_lately`), or the eyes knowing someone else. The owner's face is one the eyes know under the typed name or under the name who_you_are saved with it, matched by memory person id (`person::by_default_owner`), so a typed "Minh Nguyen" and a spoken "Minh" are one owner. A line carried as the owner's conversation going on (`LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIES`, `ScopeLease::carried_for`) keeps the name through one face the eyes don't name just now, or through no face in view; two faces, another voice or another known face still hold it back; then the name is taken out of the lamp's own earlier lines too, as a name only, never an everyday word (`person::without_name`), so its earlier answers don't carry it. No face in view, or no look, is nobody known at the lamp and holds the name back, unless the eyes knew the owner's face in the last 8 s (the owner looking away) or the line is carried as theirs (B5186, 10 Nov: an off-camera "what's my name?" with nobody in view was told the owner's typed name). The private room changes none of it. The journal says why the owner was named and where the name came from, never the name. The reply only: nothing is kept, recalled or forgotten for them on it (#4090's rule stands). A guest called by the owner's name is one word, said and corrected | on |

The lamp is a private room between it and its owner, so whoever speaks to it is the owner, and no ownership check runs: the linked services read and write for any spoken line (`services::owner_of`), and each line hears and changes every reminder, with what it sets the room's (`schedule_whose::caller`, `Caller::everyone`). The typed name is not part of it: it stays the owner's own known face's (`person::may_tell`, `someone_else_now`; B5186, 10 Nov: with nobody in view an unseen speaker was told the owner's typed name). A write's read-back stays, and whether the answer confirms or cancels is the model's to judge: it sends with the write's `send_id`, and the lamp checks only that id, a later request, two minutes and an uncut read-back (the owner, 10 Nov); a send in a line begun in the lamp's echo tail with its words, held elsewhere to the asker's voice, is taken as theirs here (`alike` 1.0, the owner's acceptance of 6 Nov). | LAMP_FACES_AT_DESK_ONLY | `on` only; off; both voice routes (passed to the provider and second workers). A face in the eyes' look counts as someone at the lamp only when it is at least 0.70 sure and its box at least 0.6 of the reference face's height in the same look, the reference the tallest face at least 0.70 sure and at most 0.6 of the frame tall, so a giant false box never drops the owner; a look with a face never counts nobody (`known_faces::at_desk`, `AT_DESK_CONFIDENCE`, `AT_DESK_HEIGHT`, `AT_DESK_TALLEST`): relative, so it holds near and far. Everything that counts people at the lamp reads it (`known_faces::people_at_lamp`): the name by default's "more than one face in view" (`most_faces_lately`), the typed name's one face, the face cache's held gap, the busy room's two faces (`bus_out::faces_in_view`, `LAMP_TWO_FACES_HELD`'s looks), the join's faces (`LAMP_JOIN_CUT_WORDS`), the room's mood note, and the scope by sight's one face, no other face and alone in view (`SpeakingFaces`: the carry, `LAMP_PERSON_SCOPE_ONE_FACE`). A face not counted is still tracked and still read speaking, so a far face seen speaking a line still makes it not the owner's, and a face not counted that read speaking in the last 10 s holds the name by default back ("a face not at the desk seen speaking", `known_faces::spoke_not_at_desk_lately`, `SPOKE_NOT_AT_DESK_WITHIN`); the eyes' own list is never changed; the early words' and follows-face's checks keep every face. The name-by-default decision line adds the deciding look's faces as `[faces counted w.. h.. c..; dropped ..]` with the switch on or a face dropped (`known_faces::faces_worth_telling`), and the scope line `N faces not at the desk`, sizes only. Owed: a real person 1 m behind the owner, untested | on | On lamp-a0ae on 2 Nov, right after setup, the owner's name was held back on 51 of 79 turns as "more than one face in view": faces 0.02 to 0.06 wide at 0.61 to 0.78 beside the owner's 0.09 to 0.19 at 0.80 to 0.92, a new track id nearly every look. On the second route, a line whose own words ask one of the staff by name to do something ("ask/tell/get/have Codex to …", "can/could you ask Claude Code …") has its first chat round sent with `tool_choice` naming `start_agent_task`, its `who` told and given only the agent the words name (a round 1 call naming another, or nobody, is given to that one); a line asking two or more is still forced, `who` told as declared, each call kept with the asked agent it names and one naming nobody or an agent not asked given to the first asked no call names yet; a mention ("is Codex done?"), a negation ("don't ask Codex to …"), a question to oneself ("should I ask Claude Code to …"), a garbled name or one not of the staff is asked as before; passed to the second worker only. A line said to someone else that names an agent becomes a prepared hand-over and a question to the room (the heard yes still guards the start). The journal says once per line, with no words: `named ask: matched <agent>`, `no match (<n> words)` or `not applied: <why>` | — | — | #1722 | probe_handover on lamp-06c1, 21 Oct, with a story told first: 30 of 30 on the three played asks (A alone also 30 of 30); not played on a lamp | off until the played hand-over test on lamp-4ace | On lamp-4ace on 21 Oct, with two of the staff, played asks of a poem by name went to `not_for_me` or were written by the lamp | until |

Opened or told only to the lamp's owner, heard (as the services hold a read); anyone else, or the model alone, is refused with nothing read (the Reviewer-lead's P1 on #6422). ## The voice's own flags (`lamp-voice serve`)

`lamp-voice`'s command line in its unit, beside its environment. Here the code's
default and the units often differ: both units already pass the lamp's choice, so
"make it the code default" is the proposal (the first builder's 07:30: a switch
that passes becomes the default in code). Missing from the first version of this
page; the Second's 07:45 on #1238.

| Switch | Values and default | lamp-4ace (repo unit) | Product unit | Added by | Lamp check it named | Proposed product default | Why (one line) | Counted as |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--turn-taking` | `vad-only` (default) / `v1-baseline` / `duplex`. `duplex`: a person heard over the lamp makes it yield | duplex | duplex | commit 7dde135af (direct) | Runs on lamp-4ace in every scene since 13 Oct (duplex-listening.md) | duplex (already); make it the code default | Both units pass it; `vad-only` is the experiment's first form | on |
| `--endpoint` | `silence` (default) / `smart-turn` / `smart-turn-fast`. The end-of-turn model judges each pause | smart-turn-fast | smart-turn-fast | commit 6ff31ca1d (direct; `smart-turn-fast` 86e5c275a) | endpoint-pilot.md: the fast model's verdict in 148 ms on lamp-4ace, before the silence window ends the turn | smart-turn-fast (already); make it the code default | Both units pass it; without the model every pause is the window's | on |
| `--noise-suppression` | `on` (default) / `off`. The capture's software noise suppression | off | off | commit 64529dee4 (direct) | Off on lamp-4ace since the voice came in | off (already); make it the code default | Both units turn it off; the code default disagrees with every lamp | on |
| `--reference-clock` | `fixed` (default) / `capture`. The echo reference follows the two USB clocks' difference | capture | capture | commit 55d7af98e (direct) | Runs on lamp-4ace since 13 Oct | capture (already); make it the code default | `fixed` is a margin a long session uses up (options.rs) | on |
| `--reference-recovery` | `off` (default) / `bounded`. Re-centres a reference whose margin was used up | off (lamp-4ace's drop-in passes `bounded` since 17 Oct morning) | bounded (#1332) | commit 55d7af98e (direct) | lamp-cb94 ran an hour clean on `bounded`, 17 Oct 07:21–08:23 UTC: no restart, no untrusted reference (Twelve, #1298); with `off` its voice ended on `ReferenceLate` at 04:53 (#1178) | bounded (#1332) | A held-up speaker no longer ends the capture worker and the voice (#1298) | on |
| `--echo-reference` | `as-given` (default) / `steady`. `steady` delays the reference to where the echo is | — | — | commit 8a494575f (direct, 14 Oct) | Played on lamp-4ace 14 Oct 18:00–19:35, six runs: with `steady` the lamp paused for nothing 35 times in 52 trials and gave up its own story 6 times, so it stays off there (steady-reference.md) | off (`as-given`) | Shown worse on the lamp; its own doc calls it an experiment, never a default | off |
| `--provider-continuation` | `off` (default) / `kept-start`. A next request's start kept back while an answer may still come | kept-start | kept-start | commit 2021c1bb9 (direct) | Runs on lamp-4ace since 13 Oct | kept-start (already); make it the code default | Both units pass it; `--early-end-ms` and the cut-off note need it | on |
| `--controls-stop` | flag; off. A hand on the button or the head stops the lamp's speech | set | set | commit 5f9914314 (direct, 14 Oct) | On lamp-4ace since 14 Oct (controls.md) | on (already) | Both units pass it; a stop by hand is the lamp's quickest control | on |
| `--early-end-ms` | 100–1000 ms with `kept-start`; off. Sends the end early after that much quiet, plays only when sure | — | — | commit af9ff59c5 (direct, 14 Oct) | Played on lamp-4ace at 200 ms: worse, so switched off (the Second's work log, row 19) | off | The end-of-turn model (`--endpoint smart-turn-fast`) took its place | off |
| `--call-wait-ms` | milliseconds; off. An utterance that is only the lamp's name ("Hey Lamp,") waits that long for the rest instead of ending; if nothing more comes, the model is told it was only the name (both routes; on the second, a lone name with no words back is asked as `Lamp.`, #1712) | — (lamp-4ace's drop-in passes 2000) | 2000 (#1332) | #891 | Runs on lamp-4ace with 2000 through 17 Oct. On #828 at 13:03 it was cleared as the cut-in regression's cause, 6 of 10 yields with the wait on against 7 of 10 off; at 12:4x, on five cut-ins, the yields were slower with it on (1.6–2.0 s against 0.5–0.9 s) | 2000 (#1332) | A person who says the name and pauses is waited for, not answered with "Yes?" | on |

## The other parts: body, eyes, expression, tasks, controls and the updater

Body flags all say "keep as is until #1192's guard is on the lamp": on 17 Oct a
body exit wrote a bad elbow goal on lamp-4ace, #1192 now refuses such goals, and
its lamp-4ace run is Monday's, with a person there.

| Switch | Values and default | lamp-4ace (repo unit) | Product unit | Added by | Lamp check it named | Proposed product default | Why (one line) | Counted as |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lamp-update` LAMP_UPDATE_SELF | `on`; off unless set. lamp-update installs the lamp-update a build carries and switches to it, and the new one must confirm itself within its deadline or `lamp-update-back` puts the old one back; a build that needs a newer updater is climbed to first, its updater alone taken (updates.md, "lamp-update's own switch") | — | on (`lamp-update.service`) | #1680 (`8390af6ab6`) | Shown on lamp-9adf on 30 Oct: the climb of 8899 → 8999 (`build 8999 needs updater 9; …`, then `confirmed:` 21.6 s later and the parts in the same serve) and the self-switch ending 8999 → 9063 (`build 9063 runs; lamp-update switches …`, `confirmed:` 0.7 s later). Not shown on a lamp: a put-back by `lamp-update-back` | on (already) | Every lamp takes its updater's own fixes over the air, and an old updater can still read a newer build | on |
| `lamp-update` LAMP_UPDATE_APPLY | `on` or `off`; unset is on only for a managed, self-updating product install with a level-6 carrier, else off ("Product update settings", below). A round's switch writes the build's signed settings and tmpfiles through `lamp-update-apply`, keeping a file the lamp changed itself (updates.md, "Settings and tmpfiles") | — | on (`lamp-update.service`) | #1680 (`4c86c98b57`) | Shown on lamp-9adf on 30 Oct 23:39:26 (8899 → 8999): `wrote /etc/tmpfiles.d/lampos.conf`, `wrote /etc/lampOS/setup/reset-plan.json`, and `kept /etc/lampOS/controls/controls-pins.json: this lamp's own`; every other setting's SHA-256 the build's after (Fourteen, #3997, 31 Oct 07:31). Not read on a lamp: what `systemd-tmpfiles` made of the new conf | on (already) | A build's settings change with the build, and a lamp's own edits stay | on |
| `lamp-update` LAMP_UPDATE_MODELS | `on`; off unless set. Not set in the environment at all, the models switch file a round carries, `/etc/lampOS/models.env` (`LAMP_UPDATE_MODELS=on`, a signed, hash-pinned setting from updater 11), decides instead; any value set in the environment, `off` included, wins over the file, and a missing or garbled file is off. A round fetches the models a build carries (`deploy/product/models.json`) that the lamp lacks, checks each by size and SHA-256 before it is named, and places it beside its part's binary or as a link into `/opt/lampOS/models/`, all or none; off, a build with another model waits for it (updates.md, "Models in the manifest") | — | on (`lamp-update.service`, 4 Nov, same-voice step 2) | #1680 | Tests only (`tests/round.rs`). Measured on lamp-9adf on 4 Nov before it: 14 of the 15 pinned models match by size and SHA-256, and only CAM++ (29,596,978 B) is missing, with 51G of 58G free. Not shown on a lamp: a round that fetches it. No round switches the updater's own unit, so a lamp in a home gets it from the carried file instead (tests only: `tests/round/models_file.rs`) | on (same-voice step 2, docs/same-voice.md) | The same-voice check's CAM++ reaches a lamp with its build | on |
| `lamp-update` LAMP_HARNESS_AT_ONBOARDING | Set: on only when `on` exactly, any other value off. Unset: on on a product lamp (the build's own default, `PRODUCT_DEFAULT`, on since 5 Nov, #5197), off on any other lamp. After each `serve` round that reached the channel, a lamp with nothing at `/usr/local/bin/harness` (or a link whose target is gone) gets Harness by `harness-on`'s install steps, run by the one-shot `lamp-update-harness.service` started without waiting: installed, not at boot, no daemon; lamp-tasks started again. Optional pin: `LAMP_HARNESS_MANIFEST_URL` with `LAMP_HARNESS_MANIFEST_SHA256` (another sha256 installs nothing). On a product lamp, unset also takes the build's compiled-in pin, and nothing unpinned is installed (#5229); `off` in a drop-in still turns it off. Every pinned install runs the build's own sha256-checked copy of Harness's installer from a file, never the CDN's, with its Node runtime, tmux, grid and hn manifests the build's copies too, served on 127.0.0.1; its OpenCode step never runs. The install's state goes to the app as `harness` in `update.status`; a failed one is tried again after the next round, an `installer` or `no_user` failure only 30 min after it, a `serve` failure at the next round (install.md, "Harness before the sign-in") | — | on (unset) | #5197's PR | a played first-onboarding run on a lamp with the switch on: Harness installed with no hand after Wi-Fi, the app showing `installing` then `installed`; shown on lamp-c0a4 5 Nov at #5379's `0e69f33bc5` (the pinned 0.5.39 installed, then signed in from the app, [#5379](https://github.com/autonomous-ai/lampOS/pull/5379#issuecomment-6656681463)); a failed install retried from the app not run | on (5 Nov): the PM's call, once the app's sign-in screen is in a TestFlight build, with the pin of #5229; the played first onboarding is the switch-on's pull request's lamp check (crates/update/src/harness_install/product.rs, `PRODUCT_DEFAULT`) | Every lamp gets Harness before its sign-in, with no shell on the lamp | on |
| `lamp-update` LAMP_HERMES_ON_LAMP | Set: on only when `on` exactly, any other value off (`off` turns a product lamp off). Unset: on on a product lamp (the build's own default, `PRODUCT_DEFAULT`, on since the product owner's decision, 10 Nov), off on any other lamp. Read only with Harness before the sign-in on. After each reached `serve` round, a lamp without Hermes as this build puts it (`hermes` in `/var/lib/lampos-hermes`, its checkout at the pin, the wrapper `/usr/local/bin/hermes` and `/usr/local/lib/lampos/hermes-ns` with this build's text) gets Hermes (NousResearch hermes-agent `v0.22.2`, commit `d923a5eb487a`), run by Harness's one-shot `lamp-update-harness.service` right after Harness: the tag's own installer, the build's sha256-checked copy, run stage by stage with `--commit` as `lampos` (Harness's user), in `/var/lib/lampos-hermes` (`lampos`'s, 0700), uv and its Python there; no other user, no group, no sudo. First `lampos` must be able to make a user namespace (`no_userns` otherwise, nothing installed), and under 1 GiB `MemAvailable` is `no_memory`, nothing installed. Hermes's own update check, plugin update check and language servers are set off in its config. The wrapper reads lamp-cloud's forwarder token (fails closed without it) and runs Hermes only in a user, mount and PID namespace with its own `/proc`, where it sees only its own folder, its work folder `/var/lib/lampos-tasks/harnesses/lamp-tasks` and the skills (read-only) over empty read-only `/run`, `/var/lib` and `/etc/lampOS`, with its own `/tmp`, no camera, mic or serial device and no lamp process visible. A Hermes of another commit there is moved aside and removed once the new one runs. For the install, lamp-update's own unit alone decides; the product's lamp-tasks and lamp-cloud units set their own switch of the same name, which sends voice work to it (rows below). A failure or the switch off takes the wrapper out; the folder stays. A failure never stops Harness or the round and is tried again 24 h later; the state is `hermes-install.json` in lamp-update's records. The schedule (the lead's rule, V2 15:44 on #5978): it starts in any reached round, the first too, with no quiet stretch and no night window, only never while the setup walk is under way; while it runs, `serve` freezes the one-shot (its own `cgroup.freeze`) as soon as the voice is not quiet and thaws it once the voice has rested 5 s, stops it after 40 min frozen in all (`interrupted`), and stops it at once when the voice's privacy lease is lost (`privacy_lost`); both are tried again in the next round. | `lamp-tasks` LAMP_TASKS_RESUME_CLOSED | `on` exactly, in lamp-tasks' own unit; off. Only with `--reply-named`. A named session that `harness ps --all` lists stopped, found by its name or folder or by its last ask (`harness show`, the 4 newest stopped ones, #5978), or whose `harness reply` says `NOT_RUNNING` (closed or stopped), is reopened once with `harness resume --machine <id> <agent> --json` (openharness#3606, Harness CLI 0.5.98 or newer) inside the hand-over's one bound. Before the first resume of a start, lamp-tasks asks once whether the installed Harness has it (`harness --help` lists `resume`, or `harness resume --help` exits 0) and keeps the answer; without it, one journal line `Harness <version> has no resume` and `session_closed`, as with the switch off. Of the answer only `ok`, `id`, `resumed` and `state` are read, never `effect`; `idle` is typed into again, `working` is waited for (`harness wait`) first; under 1 s left is `sessions_timeout`. Typed into, it is `joined` with `resumed` kept on the task, and Task news adds "I reopened that session" (`joined_reopened`), or that it starts fresh without the earlier conversation (`joined_fresh`, `resumed: false`). `RESUME_UNCONFIRMED` is `not_sent` with `session_resume_unconfirmed` (the closed-session line and "try again in a moment", `failed_closed_soon`); any other end (`NOT_YOURS`, `REPLY_OFF`, `UNKNOWN_HARNESS`, any failure or unreadable answer) is `session_closed`; still working or asking at the bound is `session_busy`. Never resumed twice in one hand-over, never `new`. Off, a closed one is `session_closed` | — | on (#5978) | #5535 | Tests (lamp-tasks' fixture Harness: each answer, an old Harness without `resume`, the look kept; the voice's words) | on (11 Nov): the owner's ask (#5978, #6349), with the first builder's default-on read on #6349 and the Harness pin at 0.6.16 (#6316); the closed-session reopen played on lamp-c0a4 on #6094 | The first builder's ruling on #5784's follow-up: a closed named session is reopened once `harness resume` ships, not answered with a dead end | on |

Reads each servo's own stored limits at start; a home, stand or pitch window outside them, or a folded arm more than 80 outside them, is refused, and a head or base goal outside them (less 20) ends the run on `Stop::Window`, unless it takes a joint lying past that window back toward it from where it lies, inside the range itself (#3504); a hold where a joint already is (take hold, rest, settle, stop pushing) is checked against the range itself. A failed watch is started again after 3, then 10 s, and given up after 3 in a row; a usage error (a Harness without `watch`) turns it off for the run. Each machine's `status` (`running`, `offline`, `unknown`, `payment_pending`) and each engine's `installed`, `installable` and `signedIn` are kept with when each was read, in `targets.engines.json` beside the targets (read one release back; 15 minutes old is unknown). Never `new`: `not_sent` with `session_not_found`, `session_ambiguous`, `session_stopped`, `session_busy`, `session_closed` (the reply's `NOT_RUNNING`: closed or stopped; nothing started on its own), the reply's other refusals (`reply_off`, `reply_not_idle`, `reply_failed`) or `sessions_…`; a reply with no answer, or taken with an answer that can't be read or names another session, is `uncertain` (`reply_uncertain`). Off, a named session is `not_sent` with `reply_named_off`. A failure with no join not acked within 5 s makes the MQTT link again. Answers the app's `update.status` for its Lamp 2 page from `lamp-update`'s own kept `update/status` value, never triggering an update or keeping any state of its own: the build it runs, its round's phase now (`idle`, `checking`, `fetching`, `switching`, `watching`, `restoring`), and the last round's outcome by its name and build. The value is said again every minute and stale three minutes after the last; `now` is `unknown` before `lamp-update` has said anything. With it the app's Check for update, `update.check` (#4153), is answered and named in `can` too: lamp-cloud says one `update/check` (`lamp_msgs::UpdateCheck`) that `lamp-update serve --apply on` reads between rounds, and its next round starts then instead of after `--every-s`, with the round's own lock and quiet moment, so a tap never installs mid-conversation. The reply is `update.status`'s object with `"check": "started"`; refused with `a round is running` (`now` not `idle` or `unknown`), `asked less than a minute ago`, or `lamp-update is not listening for a check` (no running lamp-update reads checks: an older one, a dry `serve`, or none); `serve` also drops a check that came during a round and takes at most one a minute. Without it, `not on this lamp: update.status` and `not on this lamp: update.check` (#2194, #4153, `cloud.md`, "The lamp's software update") | — | on | #2271, #2360 (#2194); #4153's PR | from the app on a lamp with it: the page through each phase, `restoring` and `stale` included — #2412's lamp-0c89 steps 1 to 8 | on: lamp-0c89 (#2194's steps, in part, #1396); it only read lamp-update's status before #4153; `update.check` is not yet run on a lamp | The app's update page is built against it; `lamp-update` already publishes the phase on every product lamp's own update path regardless, only these answers are behind the switch. Off in `lamp-skills` (or its manage helper), a skill with a write step or a `"write"` permission does not load. `lamp-services` sends it only with `LAMP_SERVICES_WRITE=on` too, when the owner's yes (`skill_write_approve`, from the voice's yes path only, never the app, which has none, nor `lamp-skills` nor the voice's model; who said yes is not checked) holds that content's SHA-256 for that skill's installed package under the same link (the account, or the link's own id; unlinking drops the yes, another account stops the skill's writes; an updated or removed skill waits for a new yes; a GitHub repository's visibility is read at the yes and said, and one approved as private but public at the send waits), and not signed out since a wipe; at most 3 sends a skill and 10 across all skills in any 24 hours, an unreadable ledger or one with a send after now counting as full. Install approves nothing ("waiting for the owner's yes"). The journal holds counts and outcomes only; the owner's sent-log (`skill_writes_sent`, the last 20, a recipient's local part only, no text) is for the voice to tell.
