| `LAMP_SKILL_TASKS` (`lamp-skills`, `lamp-skills-manage@`, `lamp-voice`, `lamp-control`) | `on` only; off unless set, in every product unit. A skill's `do.task` step and its `grants` (#6305, #6071 PR 2): `[[do]] task = { task, run = "scripts/…" }`, alone in its table, one a skill, its script a plain file in the skill's own `scripts/`, and `grants = [...]` beside it, each one of lamp-control's grants once (never `skills`; an unknown one refuses the skill). Loads only for a skill from the Skills Store (`from_store`) or pushed by the owner's own paired computer, never one made or added on the lamp (one made there only with `LAMP_MADE_SCRIPTS`, #6307). In `lamp-voice` (passed to both routes) `make_skill`'s `read_back` says such a skill back, its task, where its script came from and each grant in plain words, read by the manage helper (`task_asks`), and its yes is taken as a write's yes is, then recorded by lamp-control (itself on, `LAMP_CONTROL=on`; `task_yes_record` on its owner's socket, from the voice's cgroup alone, only while the manage helper reads the skill as it was said back) for exactly those grants and the SHA-256 of the skill's folder in its own `--state-dir`'s `task-yes.json`, never lamp-skills' state (the first builder's ruling on #6059; lamp-skills only asks, `task_yes_check`, and no other `lampos` part can write that folder: `ProtectSystem=strict` without a write path to it, or `InaccessiblePaths=-/var/lib/lampos-control` in lamp-tasks, lamp-body and lamp-eyes); a remove takes it back (`task_yes_forget`). In `lamp-skills` the skill's other steps run at once; the task is handed to lamp-tasks (`--tasks-socket` or `LAMP_SKILLS_TASKS_SOCKET`, which no unit passes until #6306) only with lamp-control's yes for the folder as hashed at that run (a changed script asks again; lamp-control not reached hands nothing over), one at a time a skill, at most 3 a skill and 10 across all skills in any 24 hours (`task-runs.json`). A skill made by the lamp's own model may carry code of its own (#6307, #6071 PR 4, the last of the five, behind a switch of its own as the first builder's 12:45 condition 4 asks): up to 4 plain-text `scripts/` files (`.py`, `.json`, `.txt` or `.md`, at most 8 KiB each and 16 KiB in all, one or two names deep, never a leading dot), run by exactly one `task = { task, run }` step naming one of its `.py` files (#6305's `do.task`) and granted only its top-level `grants` (#6305's grants, never `skills`); packed with its two files, so the package's SHA-256 covers its code; its task words and grants said back whole (the code itself is never read out), that it runs its own code, what for, that the lamp's model wrote it and what it may use, ending "It waits for you to agree before it runs any code."; never put on at once (the app's MAKE IT leaves it off, saying why to the app, and the lamp says it back aloud as a voice make does and keeps it as asked, its yes then awaited on the second route once that line is said, `make_skill_local::app_code_asked`), but on the person's later spoken yes, through the write's own wait and every guard of it: that package put on by its SHA-256 (`made_skill::put_on_agreed`), then, with `LAMP_SKILL_TASKS` on, its task's yes recorded by lamp-control (`task_yes_record`, as any `do.task`'s, never lamp-skills' state) for the SHA-256 of its folder as it went on and the grants said back, only while the skill of that name went on as that very package (`make_skill_local::code_approve`), so a changed script asks again. ## In plain words

What a person would notice with each switch on, one plain line per switch counted `until` or `now`. The stable notes' "Built but not on yet" list prints these lines instead of the rows' own words, which go under "For developers" there (`lampos-promote`'s notes, the first builder on #3976). A switch the notes list needs a line here (`on_this_repository_each_listed_switch_has_a_plain_line`): one sentence of at most 110 characters, with no switch names, flags or code. A switch that changes nothing a person would notice says so.

| Switch | What a person would notice |
|---|---|
| `LAMP_ADDRESSED` | The lamp checks whether each request was said to it from close by. |
| `LAMP_APP_SCHEDULE` | The app lists the timers and reminders you set by voice, and can cancel them. |
| `LAMP_APP_ONBOARDING_DONE` | The app follows each setup step as it ends, and opens Skills for you once setup ends. |
| `LAMP_SLEEP_HEARS_NAME` | A lamp you put to sleep with a hand on its head still wakes when you call its name. |
| `LAMP_SLIDE_WAKES` | Closing the privacy slide puts the lamp to sleep, and opening it wakes the lamp, with no touch on its head. |
| `--sleep-source` | Nothing you'd notice alone: lets the lamp listen for its name while it sleeps. |
| `LAMP_ATTENTION_NEAR_CALIBRATED` | In a busy room, the lamp knows how loud you sound from your own chair, measured at setup. |
| `LAMP_HARNESS_AT_ONBOARDING` | A new lamp installs Harness by itself once it is online, so the app can sign it in. |
| `LAMP_HERMES_ON_LAMP` | The lamp installs a coding agent of its own at a quiet time, so it can do a task when no computer is online. |
| `LAMP_HERMES_SKILLS` | The lamp's own coding agent can use the lamp's skills, as the voice does, but not while the lamp is private. |
| `LAMP_OPENCLAW_ON_LAMP` | The lamp installs OpenClaw, its default coding agent, at a quiet time, so it can do a task when no computer is online. |
| `LAMP_OPENCLAW_SKILLS` | The lamp's OpenClaw can use the lamp's skills, as the voice does, but not while the lamp is private. |
| `LAMP_LAMP_ENGINE` | Nothing you'd notice: picks OpenClaw or Hermes for work done on the lamp itself, OpenClaw unless set. |
| `LAMP_APP_ENGINES` | The app shows the lamp's agents, and you can install a missing one with a tap. |
| `LAMP_HARNESS_HOLD_UPDATES` | A home lamp keeps the Harness it was given, a test lamp takes Harness's updates; both check it is up. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_RESUME_CLOSED` | If the session you name for work was closed, the lamp reopens it and hands the work over. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH_SOCKET` | Nothing you'd notice: opens the speech connection early to try to start talking sooner. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH_SOCKET_MODEL` | Nothing you'd notice: picks the voice engine for the early speech connection, keeping one voice per answer. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_CHAT_SECOND_TRY` | When a reply is slow to start, the lamp asks once more, so fewer turns end with "my thoughts are slow". |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_KEEPS_LISTENING` | The lamp still stops if you keep talking over it, even after two earlier pauses came to nothing. |
| `LAMP_ONSET_FADE` | The lamp lowers its voice the moment you start talking over it, so it hears you better. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_NEAR_ASKER` | While answering you, the lamp no longer stops for quieter talk from farther back in the room. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_OVER_BED` | After three ignored turns, only "stop", its name, a tap or the button stops it; needs stop words always on. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_WORDLESS_ASKS` | If a sound with no words cuts its answer, the lamp says "Um, excuse me, did anyone say something?", so you know a noise cut it off; on in the product, and now and then it can ask after its own echo. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_WORDLESS_RESUMES` | If a sound with no words cuts its answer in a noisy room, the lamp picks up where it was. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_PROMPT_LOG` | Nothing you'd notice: on a test lamp, logs what it was asked after you cut in, your words among it. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_LATE_YIELD` | A question you ask over the lamp's story stops it, even when it heard you only near the end. |
| `LAMP_CARRIED_LINES_KEEP_TALK` | A long talk is remembered as one talk, even while the lamp can't see who is speaking for a while. |
| `LAMP_CONVERSATION_CARRIED_MEMORY` | Mid-talk, while only your face is in view, the lamp recalls and saves for you, but never forgets anything. |
| `LAMP_HELD_WORDS_SEEN` | When a cough or knock cuts you off just after you spoke to the lamp, it still answers what you said as yours. |
| `LAMP_BACKCHANNEL_DEBUG` | Nothing you'd notice: for debugging, it logs short replies like "yeah" said while the lamp talks. |
| `LAMP_ASIDES_HOLD` | Short asides like "wow" or "hmm, interesting" while the lamp talks no longer stop its story. |
| `LAMP_CLOUD_TLS` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp's connection to the app's server is encrypted. |
| `LAMP_CUT_INS_NOT_TALK` | Lines said over the lamp's answer and left unanswered no longer count toward a busy room. |
| `LAMP_FACE_GAP_HOLD` | If you turn away briefly mid-chat, the lamp keeps the conversation as yours for up to 12 seconds. |
| `LAMP_VOICE_SHORT_LINE_SPEAKER` | A quick "yes" you say to the lamp counts as yours when it saw you say it. |
| `LAMP_FAINT_FLOOR_MAX_DB` | The level below which the lamp ignores faint sound can adjust to the room, up to a set limit. |
| `LAMP_FOLLOWS_ON` | Outside a busy room, a line said within 20 seconds of the lamp finishing can be taken as a follow-up. |
| `LAMP_GOING_ON_NOT_OWN` | Other people's talk after the lamp answers you no longer keeps the lamp joining in, line after line. |
| `LAMP_GOING_ON_ASKS` | In a busy room, the lamp takes up to 2 seconds to decide if an unnamed follow-up is meant for it. |
| `LAMP_GOING_ON_SAME_ONE` | In a busy room, unnamed follow-ups are ignored unless they answer the lamp or come in the same voice. |
| `LAMP_NAME_OR_FOLLOW_UP` | In a busy room, facing the lamp isn't enough: say its name, unless you follow up within 8 seconds. |
| `LAMP_SLEEP_ASKED` | You can ask the lamp to go to sleep: it says so and sleeps, as when you hold its head. |
| `LAMP_HOLDING_ANSWER` | If the lamp only says a short "let me check", it comes back once with the real answer. |
| `LAMP_LISTEN_GAPS` | The lamp leaves a short pause between sentences so a "stop" said over it can be heard. |
| `LAMP_LIVE_SCOPED_SESSION` | When another known person speaks, the lamp starts a fresh chat for them, though their first line may be lost. |
| `LAMP_MISHEARD_HUM` | If the lamp mishears you cutting in, it gives a quick "hm?" and listens instead of asking "Sorry?". |
| `LAMP_NAME_NOTE_HEARD_AS` | The lamp knows its name can sound like "Mom", "man" or "ma'am", so such calls may still reach it. |
| `LAMP_OTHERS_ANSWER` | A "yes" said over the lamp's answer, not to its question, is taken as someone else's and not answered. |
| `LAMP_OUTPUT_CAPS` | Night-time limits on the light and volume stay in force, even after a restart. |
| `LAMP_REACH_PROBE` | When only the lamp's service is down but the internet works, the lamp no longer says it's offline. |
| `--reach-idle` | The lamp notices a lost internet connection within about 30 seconds, even when nobody is talking. |
| `LAMP_REMARKS` | In a busy room, the lamp ignores remarks to others like "She said it might rain" that ask it nothing. |
| `LAMP_RESEND_LOST_LINE` | If the connection drops just after you speak, the lamp sends your request once more instead of losing it. |
| `LAMP_ROOM_FILE` | After a quick restart, the lamp remembers whether the room was quiet or busy instead of starting unsure. |
| `LAMP_SERVICE_DOWN_LINE` | When the lamp's service is down but the internet works, the lamp says so once instead of going silent. |
| `LAMP_PRIVACY_GAP_RIDE` | A split-second glitch reading the privacy switch no longer ends your conversation. |
| `LAMP_VOICE_LOCK_CODE` | The lamp keeps its voice's code in memory, so a busy memory card can't freeze it mid-answer. |
| `LAMP_PRIVACY_RIDES_STALL` | A split-second freeze of the lamp cuts its answer but no longer ends the voice and mutes it. |
| `LAMP_NIGHT_ASLEEP_FACE` | Asleep at night with the light off, the lamp shows the same soft warm glow as when you put it to sleep. |
| `LAMP_NIGHT_STAYS_FOLDED` | Asleep at night, the lamp stays curled up while people talk nearby; its name or a tap still wakes it. |
| `LAMP_RING_STOP_ENDS_IT` | Say "stop" over a timer's ring and it stays stopped, instead of ringing once more. |
| `LAMP_REMINDER_PULSE` | While the microphones are off, the lamp's light pulses softly once a minute when a reminder is due. |
| `LAMP_BODY_ASK` | When the lamp's arm needs a hand, the lamp says so aloud once, and again when you call its name. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_REFUSED_HOLD` | When the voice service refuses the lamp, it says so once and retries each minute instead of going silent. |
| `LAMP_SIGNED_OUT` | A lamp removed and added again in the app says it needs setting up again, instead of going silent. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH` | Everything the lamp says, reminders and skills included, comes in one chosen voice. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_GEMINI_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sets the server address one of the speech services is reached through. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_DEEPSEEK_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sends the lamp's chat through the company's server, so no separate key is needed. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sets the server address the lamp's speech goes through. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_LISTENING_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sets the server address used to turn your speech into words. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_KEY_TERMS` | The lamp listens out for chosen words, like a city name, so it hears them right. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FIRST_WORDS` | The lamp can start speaking an answer after its first few words, rather than waiting for a full phrase. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FLUX` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp tries a newer service for turning your English speech into words. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FACE_NAME` | When the lamp knows your face and sees you ask, it can say your name back; someone off camera is never told it. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_CHECKING_LINE` | When the lamp looks something up, it says "Let me check." first instead of going quiet. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_MEMORY_HITS` | The lamp checks what it remembers against each thing you say, so its answers can use those memories. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_QUIET_HELD` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp holds back silence after you pause instead of sending it off. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_PREPARED` | The lamp starts working on its answer as you finish speaking, so it can reply sooner; nothing is said early. |
| `LAMP_NAME_AS_A_SENTENCE` | Saying "Lamp." alone before a question calls the lamp if it heard its name, even if the text got it wrong. |
| `LAMP_NAME_SAID_TO_A_PERSON` | The lamp no longer takes "Ma'am," or "Dad," at a line's start as its name, unless you face or talk with it. |
| `LAMP_NAME_START_USUAL` | Nothing you'd notice: makes sure the lamp's name said first in a line is still caught as a call. |
| `LAMP_NAME_REPEAT_NOT_A_CALL` | A desk remark about the lamp that the transcript repeats is no longer taken as calling it. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FEW_TOOLS` | Nothing you'd notice: answers start with fewer abilities loaded; remembering takes one extra step. |
| `LAMP_DECLINE_SILENT` | When the lamp decides a line wasn't said to it, it stays silent and doesn't start a conversation. |
| `LAMP_SAME_SPEAKER_CARRIES` | In a busy room, the lamp keeps talking with the same person by face and voice, and lets others' lines pass. |
| `LAMP_BUSY_CALLED_STRICT` | In a busy room with nobody facing it, the lamp ignores words that only sound like its name, like "Mom". |
| `LAMP_REMARK_ABOUT_IT` | In a busy room, the lamp stays quiet when people talk about it, like "that lamp is cute," rather than to it. |
| `LAMP_REMARK_GLANCE` | When people talk about the lamp in a busy room, it gives them one quick glance, at most once a minute. |
| `LAMP_OPENS_FURTHER` | When you share how you feel, the lamp sometimes adds a small caring line or offer, never after a command. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SKILL_LOOK` | When a skill asks it to look for something, the lamp says "Let me look." and looks right away. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_LOOK_NOTE` | While searching for something, the lamp is prompted to try the next spot if a look found nothing. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_LOOK_WALK` | While searching for something you asked for, the lamp checks up to five spots itself, then kindly gives up. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_WRITTEN_WAIT` | The lamp waits up to 7 seconds, not 3, to start its own news or reminders instead of dropping them. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_KNOWN_NAMES` | The lamp better hears the name given at setup and the helpers it hands work to, so they're misheard less. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_CUE_AFTER_MS` | The lamp's little waiting sound can come later, so it doesn't "hmm" just before an answer begins. |
| `LAMP_QUIET_GOING_ON_SAME_ONE` | In a quiet room mid-conversation, the lamp ignores a line it can tell, by voice or face, is someone else's. |
| `LAMP_USE_SKILL` | The lamp can use many skills you install without losing any of its own abilities. |
| `LAMP_CONTROLS_WAIT` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp does less busy work while idle, and touches and presses still stop it at once. |
| `LAMP_CAPTURE_WAIT` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp's microphone handling does less busy work while it waits for sound. |
| `LAMP_QUIET_EVIDENCE` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp skips a search for its own echo while it has said nothing. |
| `LAMP_STOP_HEARD` | A dedicated listener for "Stop" pauses the lamp's reply the moment you say it. |
| `LAMP_STOP_WORDS_HOLD` | The lamp no longer stops itself when it says the word "stop" in its own reply. |
| `LAMP_STOP_WORDS_KEYTERM` | The lamp listens harder for stop words like "Stop," "Wait" and "Hold on" said to it. |
| `LAMP_STOP_WORDS_GAIN_DB` | The lamp turns up what it hears of your voice while it speaks, to catch a quiet "Stop" better. |
| `LAMP_STOP_WORDS_COMMIT_WAITS` | When you say "Stop" over the lamp, it says "Okay, I'll stop there." instead of just going quiet. |
| `LAMP_TRANSCRIPTION_LANGUAGE` | Nothing you'd notice: tells the lamp which language you speak, to help it write down what it hears. |
| `LAMP_TURNED_AWAY` | In a busy room mid-conversation, the lamp ignores lines meant for others, like "Mom" or "pass me that". |
| `LAMP_UNTIED_GOING_ON` | Mid-conversation, talk with no tie to what the lamp just said no longer keeps the lamp joining in. |
| `LAMP_UNTIED_ANSWER_GRACE` | What you say right after the lamp answers you is taken as said to it, even with people talking nearby. |
| `LAMP_UNTIED_CALLER_SPEAKING` | Once you're talking with the lamp, what it sees you say is taken as said to it, even after colleagues talk. |
| `LAMP_TURNED_SPEAKING` | In a busy room, someone facing the lamp but not speaking no longer counts as talking to it. |
| `LAMP_TURNED_FACE_SPEAKS` | Someone facing the lamp but silent while others talk isn't taken as talking to it; the room counts as busy. |
| `LAMP_TWO_FACES_HELD` | A second face seen just once, like a passer-by or a photo, no longer makes the lamp treat the room as busy. |
| `LAMP_ENDPOINT_PAUSE_JOIN` | When you pause mid-sentence to think, the lamp waits a moment and answers what you said as one request. |
| `LAMP_WORKER_SCHEDULING` | Nothing you'd notice: shares the lamp's processor more fairly between its voice and its other parts. |
| `LAMP_UNCLEAR_HUM` | When the lamp can't make out what you said, it gives a quick "hm?" and a listening look instead of silence. |
| `LAMP_VAD_INPUT` | The lamp listens differently for your voice while it talks, to catch a "Stop" said over it. |
| `LAMP_OWN_LINE_KEEPS` | Room noise no longer cancels the lamp's own hellos, reminders or news before they start; its name still does. |
| `LAMP_MOTION_KEEPS_ANSWER` | The sound of the lamp's own motors while it thinks no longer interrupts its answer. |
| `LAMP_MOTION_VOICE` | The lamp tries to tell your voice from its motor noise when you speak while it moves. |
| `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_CALLER_ONLY` | The lamp learns a conversation's voice only from the person who called it, so others' lines don't take over. |
| `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_CONFIRMS` | When the lamp can see only you, it can tell your line is yours by your voice; it never ends a chat by voice. |
| `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_FOR` | The lamp checks your voice only before a linked service sends or reads in full. |
| `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_MODEL` | The lamp checks a line is in the voice of the person it's talking with; another voice ends the chat. |
| `LAMP_VOICE_TONE` | The lamp senses how calm, excited, glad or down you sound, keeping only numbers, never your words or voice. |
| `LAMP_NO_WORDS_AGAIN` | When you call the lamp by name and it comes up empty, it tries again or asks you to repeat, never silent. |
| `LAMP_ANSWER_ASK_AGAIN` | When the lamp asks you something and can't use your answer, it asks you again instead of going quiet. |
| `LAMP_CUT_ASK_ANSWERED` | A stray word right after your question no longer makes the lamp drop it; it answers you. |
| `LAMP_NAME_MOVING_LEAST` | While the lamp plays a gesture or a dance, its motors' noise no longer wakes it as if called. |
| `LAMP_NAME_WORDLESS_BAR` | A sound that only half sounds like the lamp's name, with nothing said after it, no longer wakes it. |
| `LAMP_REQUESTED_AUDIO` | When you ask for a sound by voice, the lamp can play one of its own checked recordings after confirming. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SEEMS` | When it sees you smiling, the lamp answers in a warmer tone, without ever mentioning how you look. |
| `LAMP_HEALTH_FACTS` | Asked how it is, the lamp can say which of its parts aren't working, and keeps quiet about it otherwise. |
| `LAMP_LIGHT_KEEP` | After a restart, the lamp comes back with the light you last set instead of a default level. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_LIGHT_SPEAKING` | While the lamp speaks, the ring takes on the colour of a light a skill has set, like a warm welcome-back glow. |
| `LAMP_LIGHT_WHILE_MUTED` | You can still change the light from the app while the lamp is muted or its privacy switch is closed. |
| `LAMP_LOOK_FULL_SIDE` | Nothing you'd notice: lets the lamp take larger still pictures, meant for reading handwriting. |
| `LAMP_MAKE_SKILL` | You can ask the lamp, or type in the app, for a new skill; it writes it itself and puts it on, then tells you it's on. |
| `LAMP_APP_MAKE_SKILL` | You can ask for a new skill in the app; the lamp makes it, puts it on and tells you it's on. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_UPLOAD` | You can add a skill from a file in the app; the lamp adds it and tells you it's on. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_WRITTEN` | You can write a skill in your own words in the app; the lamp adds it and tells you it's on. |
| `LAMP_MAKE_SKILL_AGENT` | A skill the lamp can't make on the first try is handed to its own helper agent before it gives up. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_EARLY_WORDS` | If the lamp recognises you partway through a talk, what you said earlier in it is remembered as yours. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_FOLLOWS_FACE` | Save your face mid-chat and what the lamp saw you say just before is remembered as yours. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_STABLE_ID` | If you change your name with the lamp, it keeps everything it remembered about you under the new name. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_FORGET_ABOUT` | Asking the lamp to forget what you said about something forgets just those things and nothing else. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_FORGET_JUST_KEPT` | Saying "forget that" right after you ask the lamp to remember something forgets exactly that, and only yours. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_LINK_USED` | Forgetting one thing no longer takes unrelated talk from the same conversation with it. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_RECALL_V2` | You can ask the lamp what you talked about on a certain day or last week, and its recall is more precise. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_STRENGTH` | When you only said you might do something, the lamp remembers it as a maybe, not as done or booked. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_KEEP_HEARD` | When you ask the lamp to remember something, it keeps your own words rather than its rewording. |
| `LAMP_KEEP_ANSWERS_AT_ONCE` | When you only ask the lamp to remember something, it says it will at once, without the usual pause. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_NEWER_FIRST` | When you tell the lamp something has changed, it recalls your newer words before the old ones. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_REPLACES` | When you correct something the lamp remembered, it keeps the new version in place of the old one. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_NIGHTLY` | Overnight, while the lamp is idle, what you said that day is sorted into what it remembers about you. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_NIGHTLY_PATTERNS` | Overnight, the lamp can also notice things you do again and again, across days. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_NIGHTLY_FEELINGS` | Overnight, the lamp can also keep how you said you felt, in your own words. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_PROFILE` | A new talk starts with the lamp knowing your tastes and habits, even after many open plans. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_ROLE` | Tell the lamp "you are Harry Potter" and it keeps that name with you, days later too, until you end it. |
| `LAMP_SERVICE_CONTACTS` | Spell a friend's address once and send; later "mail Octavia" fills it in, until you ask the lamp to forget it. |
| `LAMP_SERVICE_RECORDS` | Every mail, event or search you ask of a linked service is kept with how it went, until you say to forget it. |
| `LAMP_SERVICE_RECORDS_TOLD` | Ask "did you do it?" and the lamp answers from what really happened with your mail, event or search. |
| `LAMP_SERVICE_REDO` | Say "do it again at 8 PM" after a cancelled event, and the lamp redoes it from what it kept, read back first. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_REOPEN_QUIET` | After a quiet spell of ten minutes or more, the lamp can follow up on something you told it earlier. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_AFTER_FORGET` | After you say forget everything, the lamp holds back shared notes about people from you for the day. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_SPEAKER` | When you say forget everything, the lamp also forgets what it saw you say to the whole room. |
| `LAMP_NAMES_RECORD_TOLD` | If the lamp saved your face under a misheard name, it says the name it kept and can still forget it. |
| `LAMP_NAMES_RENAME_YES` | Saying yes in your own words to a new name renames you, and the lamp never says it did when it didn't. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_REMEMBER_ASKED` | Asking the lamp to remember your face while giving your name saves them straight away. |
| `LAMP_NAMES_RENAME_ASKED` | If the lamp knows you and you tell it another name, it asks before calling you by the new one. |
| `LAMP_ONBOARDING_NUDGE` | During setup, the lamp moves through the steps more reliably, catching your name and where you sit. |
| `LAMP_ONBOARDING_FIRST_TRIES` | At the end of setup, the lamp suggests a few things to try first, such as a timer or the weather, once. |
| `LAMP_ONBOARDING_HARNESS_LINE` | At the end of setup, the lamp says once, in its closing words, how to let it take on tasks from your computer. |
| `LAMP_FACE_EXPRESSIONS_ASK` | During setup, the lamp asks once if it may notice whether people look happy or upset, to answer more warmly. |
| `LAMP_PLACE` | Set for one lamp, it knows the town it's in, so weather and nearby questions are answered for there. |
| `LAMP_PERSON_NAME_OWNER_ONLY` | The lamp uses the owner's name only when it sees the owner alone, so visitors aren't called by it. |
| `LAMP_FACES_AT_DESK_ONLY` | Small or unsure faces, far behind you or on a poster or a screen, no longer count as someone else here. |
| `LAMP_ONE_FACE_REMINDERS` | Alone in view, asking "what reminders do I have?" lists your own reminders even when the lamp didn't see your lips move. |
| `LAMP_PERSON_SCOPE_ONE_FACE_READS` | Alone in view, your lips unseen, the lamp recalls your memory for you, but never forgets or changes anything. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_NAME_ONE_FACE` | Alone in view, asking "what's my name?" gets your name even when the lamp didn't see your lips move. |
| `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS` | The lamp skips a request it judges was meant for someone else; changes may take up to 2 seconds longer. |
| `LAMP_FOLLOWS_RUN` | Right after the lamp speaks, a follow-up without its name can still change the light or the volume. |
| `LAMP_PROGRESS_NEWS` | While a longer task runs, the lamp may say a short sentence about how it's going. |
| `LAMP_PROVIDER_WRITES` | Nothing you'd notice: keeps the voice from dropping on a slow network, though answers may come a bit later. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_ACCOUNTS` | Questions about your calendar or email go to your computer's agent instead of the lamp's own reminders. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_QUESTIONS` | A question about one of your computer's agents is passed to that agent to answer, not guessed by the lamp. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_SIGN_IN` | You can sign the lamp in to your computer agents by voice: it reads out a code to type and checks your email. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_SIGN_IN_APP` | You can sign the lamp in to your computers from the app, which shows the code to type. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_MACHINE_NAMES` | The lamp calls your computers by their own names when telling you which one has your task. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_NAMED_ASK` | Asking one of your computer's agents by name to do something hands the task straight to that agent. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_CORRECT` | After the lamp reads back a task, you can say no, I said, and correct it before saying yes. |
| `LAMP_TASK_NEWS_RETELL` | If you talk over the lamp telling you a task finished, it tells you once more when the room is quiet. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_NOW` | You can ask how any of your agents' recent work is going, or if one finished, and hear it at once. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_HARNESS_STATUS` | You can ask if the lamp's Harness is signed in, and which of your computers and agents it can hand work to. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_ONE_YES` | When the lamp reads back a task for your computer, one yes is enough to start it. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_REPLY_NAMED` | Name or describe earlier work on your computer ("the survey one") to open it, check it or add to it. |
| `LAMP_UNPLUG` | You can ask the lamp to fold up for unplugging; it says when it's done, and "never mind" undoes it. |
| `LAMP_PEEK` | A developer can open the lamp's live status page, by a one-time link, from a browser on the same Wi-Fi. |
| `LAMP_UNPLUG_STAY_MIN` | If nobody unplugs the folded lamp within 10 minutes, it says it's still plugged in and gets back up. |
| `--caller-caps` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp's body checks who asks it to move, so a skill can't take a person's place. |
| `--lease-socket` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp's idle moves stand back while a program on a paired computer moves it. |
| `--overload` | The arm's motors let go when straining hard for a while, then take hold again two minutes later. |
| `LAMP_BODY_OWN_RANGES` | The arm stays within its own calibrated limits and won't move a joint past them. |
| `LAMP_BODY_APP_REACH` | A program can move the lamp's joints up to 90% of the way to their hardware limits (a dev lamp only). |
| `LAMP_BODY_STRAIN_GUARD` | If the lamp's arm strains or overheats twice in an hour, it stays folded until you hold its head for a second. |
| `LAMP_BODY_POSE_ASK` | You can ask the lamp to stand lower, or back to normal, and it stays there. |
| `--steady-rate` | The lamp checks a still face half as often, saving effort but correcting its gaze a little slower. |
| `--look-on-change` | Nothing you'd notice: with nobody around, the camera skips face checks while the view hasn't changed. |
| `--change-persist` | Nothing you'd notice: a brief flicker in a dark room no longer counts as a change, saving more effort. |
| `--leave-still-faces` | The lamp ignores small, faint face-like shapes that never move, which are not real people. |
| `LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING_DROP_OUTLIER` | The lamp still notices who is talking when one camera frame of their face is misread. |
| `LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING_STEADY` | The lamp no longer takes a quiet face for talking while its own head moves, and reads lips from further away. |
| `LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING_SKIP_JUMP` | The lamp sees you talking at your desk more often, so it keeps your words as yours. |
| `LAMP_EYES_FAST_STREAM` | An app you paired with the camera allowed gets live video from the lamp, not two small pictures a second. |
| `LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING_ALL_FACES` | The lamp can tell whether other faces in view are talking, not only the nearest one. |
| `LAMP_TURN_TO_ANSWERED` | As it answers, the lamp turns its head to the one person it saw talking just before. |
| `--visits` | The lamp keeps a rough sense of how often remembered people visit, never when, and tells them it does. |
| `--photo-dir` | Someone can add a face from a photo; the lamp keeps it unconfirmed until that person says yes at the lamp. |
| `LAMP_PHOTO_FACES` | When a person added from a photo is at the lamp, it asks them once whether it may remember them. |
| `LAMP_REMEMBER_SPEAKER` | When asked to remember someone, the lamp learns only the face of the person who introduced themselves. |
| `LAMP_PHOTO_SPEAKER` | The lamp keeps a photo-added face only if the yes comes from that same person, not someone in their place. |
| `LAMP_FACES_PAGE` | The app lists the faces the lamp knows and can remove a person, along with what the lamp remembers of them. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_FORGET_SOCKET` | Removing a person in the app also erases the lamp's memories of them, without the app ever reading them. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_DONT_KNOW_RECALLS` | When the lamp would say it doesn't know something you told it, it first looks in what it kept for you. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_DOUBT_UNDOES` | When the lamp asks whether it heard a word right, it keeps the word only after you say yes. |
| `--taste-rules` | Everyday likes and dislikes you mention in passing are remembered in your own words, no "remember" needed. |
| `LAMP_SEEMS_BROW_LOW` | The lamp reads lowered brows without a smile as someone feeling low, catching long faces it misses now. |
| `LAMP_FACE_FULL_CROP` | The lamp recognises remembered faces more steadily and keeps their name through a brief miss. |
| `LAMP_FACE_KNOWN_HOLD` | The lamp keeps a remembered face's name through a brief miss, without changing how it reads faces. |
| `LAMP_FACE_KNOWN_UNSURE` | The lamp names you again only once it has seen your face, never on a guess. |
| `LAMP_FACE_REFOUND` | The lamp knows a remembered face again the moment it turns back, not up to two seconds later. |
| `--seems-log` | Nothing you'd notice: keeps a short-lived log of expression reads, never whose face, to measure accuracy. |
| `LAMP_PRESENCE` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp tracks whether anyone is at the desk, for features like the light going out. |
| `LAMP_PRESENCE_VOICE` | Chatter near the lamp no longer counts as someone at the desk; only speech said to the lamp does. |
| `LAMP_PRESENCE_FAR` | A still face far from the lamp, like someone behind or a picture, no longer counts as someone at the desk. |
| `LAMP_WHO_WAS_HERE_KNOWN` | Asking who was here can tell you that someone the lamp knows came by, without saying who. |
| `LAMP_WHO_WAS_HERE_NAMES` | Asking who was here names visitors who agreed to be named; others stay unnamed. |
| `LAMP_EYES_YAWN` | The lamp counts your yawns while you sit, only with your okay for face expressions, keeping just the count. |
| `--mouth-on-heard` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp studies mouths closely only while it hears speech, saving effort. |
| `--mouth-runtime` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp reads mouth shapes with a different, faster engine. |
| `--not-while-speaking` | The lamp no longer mistakes its own voice for someone calling its name while it talks. |
| `--quiet-below` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp rests its name listener in a silent room, saving effort, and hears the same. |
| `--rescue-model` | On test lamps only: a second listener helps the lamp hear its name more often. |
| `--stop-least` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp also listens for the word stop, ready for a feature that uses it. |
| `--offline-face` | When the lamp is offline, its light no longer shows it's ready to listen. |
| `--sign-in-by-code` | You can sign the lamp in by voice, with a code it reads out, so it can hand work to your computers. |
| `--folder-missing` | If handed-off work can't start because its folder isn't on that computer, the lamp says so plainly. |
| `--engines-ready` | Before handing off work, the lamp checks the computer's assistant is signed in and says if it isn't. |
| `--engines-from-machines` | The lamp knows which assistants each computer has, and says at once if one is missing, signed out or asleep. |
| `--route` | Work you hand off goes into the session on your computer it belongs to, not a new one each time. |
| `--reply-named` | Work you hand off can go into a session you name on your computer, not a new one. |
| `--absolute-folder` | Nothing you'd notice: work you hand off reaches your computer a little sooner. |
| `--new-folder` | Work handed to a new folder on your computer starts without waiting for you to trust it. |
| `LAMP_CONTROLS_STRIP` | The touch strip on the lamp's base responds to touches and strokes. |
| `LAMP_SLEEP_LINE` | Holding the lamp's head to put it to sleep, it says how to wake it before it stops listening. |
| `LAMP_HEAD_PET` | Petting the lamp's head gets a response and a little wiggle instead of putting it to sleep. |
| `LAMP_EARS_CLASSES` | Nothing you'd notice: with your okay, the lamp tells speech, music and laughter apart, keeping no audio. |
| `LAMP_MOOD` | The lamp has a mood: its light and head breathe quicker when lively and slower when dozing. |
| `LAMP_MOOD_TONE` | How your words sound nudges the lamp's mood, seen only in its breathing and liveliness; nothing is kept. |
| `LAMP_MESSAGES` | You can text the lamp from the app and it texts back, and it can send you news of tasks unasked. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_MESSAGES` | Guard mode and other skills can text you, with nothing else of texts turned on. |
| `LAMP_SKILLS_SHARE` | You can send a skill you made to the Skills Store; a reviewer reads it before others can add it. |
| `LAMP_SCHEDULE_EVERY` | You can ask for a reminder every few minutes or hours, as often as every 15 minutes. |
| `LAMP_REMINDERS_HIDDEN_COUNT` | Asked your reminders when it can't see you, the lamp says how many more belong to someone, never what. |
| `LAMP_APP_CONSENT` | In the app you can turn on or off whether the lamp reads faces and, on a lamp with no slide, room sounds. |
| `LAMP_APP_RESTART` | You can restart the lamp from the app; it waits for a pause in talk and never restarts during an update. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_FROM_APP` | You can turn guard mode on and off from the app, not only by voice. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_SOUNDS` | In guard mode, a door or footsteps followed by a face or speech counts as someone arriving. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_NAMES` | Guard mode's texts name a visitor the lamp knows if they agreed to be named. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_WORDS_V2` | Saying you're back or that it can stop watching turns guard mode off, instead of on. |
| `LAMP_REST_THROUGH_SOUNDS` | At night, room sounds without words no longer keep the lamp awake; words, its name or a touch still do. |
| `LAMP_YAWN_SOONER` | Once the lamp has seen you yawn, your stretch break comes at 30 minutes instead of 50. |
| `LAMP_NUDGES_PRESENT` | Water and break nudges come more often, never two within half an hour; mornings, it asks what's first. |
| `LAMP_SETTINGS_WIFI` | You can change the lamp's Wi-Fi from the app; if the new one fails, it goes back to the old one. |
| `LAMP_PRIVATE_ROOM` | The lamp treats whoever speaks to it as its owner, so it never refuses you for not recognising you. |
| `LAMP_WIFI_RECOVERY` | If the lamp can't reach its Wi-Fi, it opens setup and asks you to set its Wi-Fi again in the app. |
| `LAMP_OWN_KEYS` | You can use your own accounts for the lamp's replies and hearing instead of ours. |
| `LAMP_FIRST_VOLUME` | A new or reset lamp starts speaking at 70 % volume, not full, and keeps any level you choose after that. |
| `LAMP_LIGHT_TOLD` | Asked about its light, the lamp says the colour and brightness it really shows, never a guess. |
| `LAMP_SERVICES_TOLD` | Asked about a service you linked in the app, the lamp knows it is linked and uses it. |
| `LAMP_ACTION_CLAIM_CHECKED` | The lamp never says it changed its light or volume, set a timer or snoozed a reminder, unless it really did. |
| `LAMP_ACTION_CLAIM_WORDED` | The lamp never says in its own words it set a timer or changed its light, or until when, unless it did. |
| `LAMP_OWNER_PLACE` | The app can give the lamp your rough area, so "near me" and weather questions are answered for it. |
| `LAMP_CHOSEN_VOICE` | You can pick the lamp's voice in the app and describe its character in a few words. |
| `LAMP_CONTROL` | Nothing you'd notice: lets paired computers on your network control the lamp, for developers. |
| `LAMP_CONTROL_RATES` | Nothing you'd notice: for developers, programs may ask the lamp as often as they like; the lamp's body still keeps every move safe. |
| `LAMP_SHUTDOWN` | You can turn the lamp off from the app or a long press; it rests, shows a safe-to-unplug light and goes off. |
| `LAMP_SERVICES` | Link your mail, calendar and work apps in the app; the lamp reads them to you, its owner, when you ask. |
| `LAMP_SERVICES_WRITE` | The lamp sends a mail or post you asked for facing it, after reading it back in full and hearing you say send. |
| `LAMP_CALENDAR_EDITS` | The lamp can move or cancel a calendar event for you, after reading the change back and hearing your yes. |
| `LAMP_GMAIL_DRAFT_EDITS` | The lamp can change or delete a Gmail draft it saved, after reading it back and hearing your yes. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_READS` | A skill you make can read you a linked service, like your calendar, only when you are alone with the lamp. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_LINK_NOTE` | A skill you made that reads a service you haven't linked tells you once where to link it in the app. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_LINK_APP` | A skill that needs a service you haven't linked opens that service's Connect in the app for you. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_WRITES` | A skill you made sends one fixed thing on its own, at most three a day, once you agree, then or later. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_TASKS` | A Store or computer skill hands a longer job to the lamp's agent, using only what you agreed it may use. |
| `LAMP_MADE_SCRIPTS` | A skill the lamp makes for you can run its own code, once it tells you what it may use and you agree. |
| `lamp-tasks --push-watch` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp hears that a task on your computer is done as it ends, not at its next check. |
| `LAMP_LEDGER_REFENCE` | A lamp whose timers stopped ringing after a restore rings them again, or says it couldn't set one. |

## What the census found

Gaps between the docs, the units and the code, for whoever owns each:

- **lamp-4ace's repo unit sets less than the docs say lamp-4ace runs.**
  `docs/tasks.md` says `LAMP_TASKS_WHO` and `LAMP_TASKS_ASKED` stay on on
  lamp-4ace (15 Oct 21:30), `docs/attention.md` records
  `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS` taken there on the merged build (16 Oct 07:25),
  #997 and #1148 describe head taps (`LAMP_HEAD_TOUCH`) and the 12:20 mute
  (`--controls-mute`), #842 the full look (LAMP_LOOK_FULL with `lamp-eyes
  --full-look`, since 16 Oct 03:17), #1242 `LAMP_START_OFFLINE`, and
  second-voice.md the backup voice's config.
  `deploy/lamp-4ace/lamp-voice.service` sets none of them,
  so they live in drop-ins on the lamp that the repo doesn't have. Before
  switching groups on, the lamp's own drop-ins should be read and copied here.
- **No repo policy unit passes `--act attention` or `--act progress`; the
  product units pass `--hello --act hello --gate --hello-by-rule --rules`** (the
  first builder, 30 Oct, on the launch testers' findings and on #4625), so
  Welcome back's hello, asked of the gate even with `LAMP_SKILLS_ASK=skip`,
  is decided by the hard rules alone, one hello an hour to each person
  among them, at once; the judge is asked only in shadow and never decides
  (skills.md, policy.md). From
  the repo units alone `LAMP_PROGRESS_NEWS` does nothing, and `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS` only
  makes what it holds 2 s late: it waits for a decision that never comes,
  then goes on (`policy_holds.rs`).
- **Removed (#2825):** `LAMP_SPEAK_FIRST` and `LAMP_HELLO_ASKS_POLICY` went
  with the voice's own hello; the hello on an arrival is the Welcome back
  skill's alone ([speaking-first.md](speaking-first.md#one-hello-from-the-skill-2825)).
  A unit that still sets either changes nothing.
- **Removed (the first builder, 3 Nov, on the Queue):**
  `LAMP_MAKE_SKILL_LOCAL` went with the computer's agent as a skill maker.
  A skill is made by the lamp's own model whenever `LAMP_MAKE_SKILL=on`
  (by voice) or `LAMP_APP_MAKE_SKILL=on` (from the app), with no tasks part
  ([skills.md](skills.md#made-by-voice-1685-lamp_make_skillon-on-in-the-product));
  the product unit no longer sets it, and a unit that still does changes
  nothing.
- **lamp-tasks:** the product unit passes `--progress --push-wait`; the
  lamp-4ace unit doesn't (reverted in `4b8922bbc`, its build being older than
  `--push-wait`), while `docs/tasks.md` says lamp-4ace runs the same. Both
  units pass `--watch`, which changes nothing.
- **Head touch:** the #2948 carry pairs product Controls' `--touch`, Voice's
  `LAMP_HEAD_TOUCH`, `--controls-mute` and `LAMP_RESTORED_MUTE`, Expression's
  `--head-touch`, and pins without `legacy_head`. Install preflights these
  product units and relevant preserved overrides after its updater lock,
  before state handling, users, links, units or head files change. It uses
  the updater's literal-mode rules and refuses conflicting or unknown
  modes without rewriting local drop-ins or head files. The attended hand
  and injected-input recovery evidence above are separate. The cue default
  is -20 dBFS after the c0a4 tap-hum comparison and First's 11:40 approval
  (#3275); that ear check does not qualify every path sharing the default.
- **`LAMP_SHORT_SOUNDS=quiet-room`** is in both units, the form #800 showed
  answering a room sound; `quiet-room-checked`, which fixes it, was in neither
  until #1332 put it in the product unit.
- **Stale text:** `deploy/lamp-4ace/RUNBOOK.md` says `LAMP_ROOM_FACTS` is "off;
  not yet heard" while the unit sets it on; the "Switch" section of
  `docs/attention.md` says `busy-near` isn't on lamp-4ace while the same file
  records it on since 14 Oct 18:46; `docs/tasks.md`'s "Not shown yet" for
  #903 (`handovers.sh` ran on lamp-4ace on 16 Oct) and its "Nothing was run
  on a lamp" for #769 (its 15 Oct 17:47 minute); test comments in `lamp-body.rs` say
  `--wake`, `--rest-glance` and `--look-at-name` are off unless asked for, while
  the code defaults them on; the units' comments say the Policy's gate "is not
  built yet (#939)" while `docs/skills.md` describes it built (#958).
- **Five of the voice's own flags default in code to what no lamp runs** (`--turn-taking`, `--endpoint`, `--noise-suppression`, `--reference-clock` and `--provider-continuation`): both units pass the lamp's choice, so a lamp started without the unit's line gets the experiment's first form.
- **A product body's motion flags are partly per lamp:** `BODY_POSES` in a
  lamp's `/etc/lampOS/body/lamp.env` can carry flags, and lamp-06c1's passes
  `--torque on`.
- **Small ones:** `LAMP_SCHEDULE_SPEAK` is defined twice (`reminders.rs` and
  `tools.rs`); `LAMP_ONBOARDING` has no doc comment; #987
  (`LAMP_LOOK_FULL_SIDE`) names no lamp check.
- **`LAMP_SCHEDULE_SPEAK=skill` never speaks a reminder** (the reviewer on
  #1238, a probe test on #1038, 08:28; the Second's fix is #1253): the coordinator takes `skill` as
  speaking (`reminders.rs`), but only `on` passes `--schedule-speak` to the
  provider worker (`tools.rs`), so the worker defers every due reminder as
  disabled (`provider_worker.rs`, `reminder_policy.rs`). It came in with
  #1038. Fixed since: the worker gets `--schedule-speak` under `skill` too
  (`tools::tests::schedule_speech_by_skill_gives_the_worker_its_flag`).
- **`LAMP_KERNEL_CLOCK` never reached the provider worker:** it is spawned
  with `env_clear()` and the switch isn't passed on (`process.rs`), so the
  worker's `the_time` and session note never saw the clock unset (Ten on
  #705, 17 Oct). Fourth passes it in #1158.
- **#1332 brought the product voice unit up to lamp-4ace's** (the first
  builder, 17 October 15:41, for Sunday's reinstall and every product lamp):
  20 switches lamp-4ace ran all through 16 and 17 October, `--reference-recovery
  bounded` (after lamp-cb94's crash, #1298) and `--call-wait-ms 2000`. Their
  "Product unit" cells say "(#1332)". For several of them, this page's
  proposal was "off until" a named lamp check that no doc records yet
  (`LAMP_STAFF_NOTE`, `LAMP_TASKS_WHO`, `LAMP_LIGHT_REQUESTS`,
  `LAMP_FIRST_WORDS` among them). The first builder chose to ship them as
  lamp-4ace runs them, and the proposals stay as written, for each one's
  check. Left out of the product at that review: what needs the second voice's
  configuration, the office's own, head touch and `--controls-mute`.
  **Six more were left out at #1332's review** (Nine's P2, `a9c9153ac`),
  because the product's other parts don't pass the flags they need:
  `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS`, `LAMP_HELLO_ASKS_POLICY` (removed since with
  the voice's own hello, #2825) and
  `LAMP_PROGRESS_NEWS` need the policy unit's `--act attention`, `--hello`
  and `--act progress`, which it doesn't pass, so they would only make what
  they hold 2 s late or do nothing; `LAMP_LOOK_FULL` needs the eyes'
  `--full-look` and its file (both in the product since #2791's product half); `LAMP_RESTORED_MUTE` needs `--controls-mute`.
  `LAMP_MARK_WEIRD` is left out because a reset doesn't wipe its marks.

### Product update settings

Both switches have rows in "The other parts" above, with what a lamp has shown.
The existing `LAMP_UPDATE_APPLY` switch is explicitly `on` in the product
updater unit. Explicit `off` still prevents settings writes. When it is
absent on an older product install, automatic settings apply requires an
authenticated level-6 product carrier, `LAMP_UPDATE_SELF=on`, and a regular
base updater service using its managed `update/current` link. Other
installations and older non-product manifests keep the off default. The
settings allowlist, signed helper request and three-way local-edit policy
are unchanged; see [updates](updates.md#product-service-units-and-settings-over-the-air).
